| 321 | ama | רפסודה | `לְהָבִיא עֲצֵי אֲרָזִים מִן הַלְּבָנוֹן אֶל יָם יָפוֹא` (עזרא ג:ז) | [equivalent] | The same transport. |
| 324 | u gilgāmeš qaqqad ḫumbāba rakib-ma | וגילגמש – ראש חומבבה, רוכב ו[…] | `וַיִּקַּח דָּוִד אֶת רֹאשׁ הַפְּלִשְׁתִּי וַיְבִאֵהוּ יְרוּשָׁלִָם` (שמואל א יז:נד) | [equivalent] | **The young hero brings home the giant's head** (cf. 124 // 17:44, 286 // 17:51). |

### Lines shared with other tablets
- **SB II (first copies)**: speech formulas 31, 73, 129, 178, 196, 249, 265, 281, 302, 313 = II 216 (brackets per copy); 34, 125 = II 193; 130 = II 232 (*ammīni* for *ana mīni*, same Hebrew; brackets, opening quote); 131 = II 233 (identical); 167 ~ II 289 (`אמך ילדתך` for `לבך נשאך`, as the Akkadian).
- **SB III**: 159–165 ~ III 88–93 (precative > preterite; the list of winds distributed differently; plural *kakkū*); 49 ~ III 4–5, 9 (the elders' proverb: *pagaršu iṣṣur* as OB III 256, not III 5 *ibiršu iṣṣur*; *lišallim* = III 9 `יביא בשלום`); eBL's III 88–92 = V 137–141 is a positional false link.
- **SB VII**: 90 ~ VII 91 (*ibakki* `בוכה` for *unabbi*; Šamaš vocalized as everywhere in V); 91 = VII 92; 96 = VII 133 (brackets inverted: *tukku* preserved here); 316–317 ~ VII 44–45 (decision 5).
- **SB X**: 305 ~ X 36 (`את הנוצר קטלתם`); 286 ~ X 94 (*ina idīšu* `מצדו` for *ina šibbīšu* `מחגורו`); 61 ~ X 185 (`אומר` for `מדבר`, *iqabbi* for *izzakkar*).
- **SB I**: 4 *ittallaku* ~ I 151 *ittanallak* `מתהלך`; 317 *uppuša* ~ I 104 *uppuš* `עשוי`; 7 *parakku* ~ I 5 `דוכני־הקודש`.
- **Within the tablet**: 1 = 53 (brackets); 35 ~ 130; 90 ~ 185 = 206 = 256 = 274 (subject; brackets); 91 = 186 = 207 = 257 = 275 (brackets, final marks); 94 ~ 170; 124 ~ 193 (precative > *lū* + preterite; suffix); 194–195 = 263–264 (+ *u […]*); 197–204 = 266–272 (266 without *erēni*; 271 = 202 + 203; 272 *inarru*); 184 = 205 ~ 255 ~ 273; 253 = 254; 291 = 292; 300 ~ 306; 29–30 ~ 44.
- **OB parallels (not yet translated)**: OB Harmal₂ 5 (= 43), 14–15 (~ 4–5), 42–48 (~ 168–179); OB Ishchali 12′–39′ (~ 99, 250–251, 285–288, 307, 311–312; the *melammū*), a+1 (~ 318); OB IM 13 (~ 19, 308), 17–29 (~ 7, 300–319: *ina dunnīka-ma maṣṣaram tenēram*, the door, *ana bīti ellil libil purattum*, *liḫdū-ma ummān nippurim*). The OB translator should start from the Hebrew here and change exactly what the OB changes.

### Proposals for the shared renderings
1. **Key terms (§4)**: *ṣillu* = `צֵל` [cognate] (9, 27, 94; Ezek 31:6, Ps 91:1, 121:5); *mūšab ilī* = `מושב האלים` [equivalent ↔ Ezek 28:2 `מוֹשַׁב אֱלֹהִים`]; *ḫalbu* = `חֹרֶשׁ`, *ṣarṣaru* = `סְבַךְ` (with *qištu* = `יַעַר`; **no garden word without *kirû***); *papallu* = `שָׁתִיל`; *šurmēnu* = `שׁוּרְבִּין` (open decision 4); *naḫlaptu* = `מַעֲטֶה` and *ḫalāpu* = `התעטף` (add to the *melammu* row: SB V's auras are "seven cloaks of radiance"); *eṭēru napišta* = `מִלֵּט נֶפֶשׁ` (≠ *ezēbu* Š `הציל`); *napšatuš šeʾû* = `בִּקֵּשׁ עַל נַפְשׁוֹ` (Esth 7:7); *labāru* = `הִזְקִין` (open decision 1); *qēbiru* = `קוֹבֵר`; *napāḫu* = `נָפַח` [cognate of Gen 2:7 `וַיִּפַּח`] and *nappāḫu* = `נַפָּח`; *šušlušu* = `מְשֻׁלָּשׁ` [cognate]; *enēqu* = `יָנַק` [cognate]; *atmanu* = `דְּבִיר`; *riqqu* = `בֹּשֶׂם`; *tušāru* = `יְשִׁימוֹן`; *libbātu* = `חָרוֹן`; *alāku* Gtn = `התהלך` (I 151, V 4) ↔ Gen 3:8.
2. **Ordinary words (§5)**: *giṣṣu* = `נַעֲצוּץ`; *ḫâlu* = `נָזַל`, *ḫīlu* = `שְׂרָף`; *zanānu* G = `נִמְטַר` (Š `הִמְטִיר`); *ḫarru* = `אָפִיק`; *zizānu* = `צְרָצַר`; *šisītu* = `צְוָחָה`; *šamāru* II = `שׁוֹרֵר`; *ḫalālu* Š = `מַחֲלִיל`; *sukannīnu* = `תּוֹר` (always vocalized); *laqlaqqu* = `חֲסִידָה`; *riāšu* = `גָּל` (`יָגִיל`); *pagû* = `קוֹף`; *mīrānu* = `גּוּר`; *labû* = `צָוַח`; *nāru* / *tigû* = `מְנַגֵּן` / `מְתוֹפֵף`; *lilissu* = `תֹּף`; *kamālu* = `זָעַף`; *mašû* = `שָׁכַח`; *palālu* = `שָׁקַד`; *tākaltu* (sheath) = `נָדָן`; *lapātu* "smear" = `מָרַח` (beside "touch" `נָגַע`); *makû* = `דַּל`; *mušḫalṣītu* = `חֲלַקְלַקָּה`; *ašlu* = `אַגְמוֹן`; *ūṣu* = `שֶׁלַח`; *arkatu* = `יַרְכָתַיִם`; *ragāmu* = `רָעַם`; *nuʾû* = `בַּעַר`; *lillu* = `כְּסִיל` (§12); *raqqu* / *šeleppû* = `צַב־מַיִם` / `צָב`; *aḫû* = `זָר`; *napšāru* = `גָּרוֹן`; *nāʾeru* = `שׁוֹאֵג`; *tumru* = `רֶמֶץ`; *ištuḫḫu* = `שׁוֹט`; *šapāru* = `שָׁלַח`; *asīdu* = `עָקֵב`; *ṣalāmu* = `שָׁחַר` (vocalized); *sapānu* = `שִׁטַּח`; *unnīnu* = `תַּחֲנוּנִים`; *girûtu* = `מְרִיבָה`; *agru* = `שָׂכִיר`; *ekâm* = `הֵיכָן`; *bâru* = `לָכַד`; *rummû* = `הַרְפָּיָה` (plene `הרפייה`); *ḫašû* = `רֵאָה`; *ṭuḫdu* = `רֹב`; *dunnu* = `עָצְמָה`; *sumkīnu* = `שְׁבָבִים`; *terku* = `מַכָּה`; *muḫḫu* = `קָדְקֹד`; *mangu* = `*קִשְׁיוֹן*`; *uttūtu* = `בֶּהָלָה`; *apillû* = `כּוֹהֵן־אַפִּלּוּ`.
3. **verify.py**: add *arru* to the allowed lemmas of the ARR check (V 283 *arrāt*, eBL's lemma for the plural of *arratu*), so that `מארות` can stand; then V 283 should change to `עד מארות … […]` (open decision 2).
4. **§7 "always vocalize"**: `שָׁחַר` (black / dawn), `הַתּוֹר` (turtledove / turn), `כּוֹהֵן־אַפִּלּוּ` (`אפילו` "even"), `יִימָּלְכוּ` (consult / reign), `מַלֵּט`, `תִּסַּח`; and **plene before niqqud for Niphal / Piel forms with a written yod** (`יימלכו`, `ביתקו`, `יגוף`) – the strip test caught five.

### Lessons for the guidelines
- **A newly published manuscript can change the character of a passage** (the Sulaymaniyah tablet: the "silent" forest of the older editions is a noisy, living forest full of birdsong, Ps 104:12–17): when eBL incorporates a new witness, read its lines as a block before deciding section headings and parallels, and say in the index which rows rest on it alone.
- **Lines that eBL translates without any witness of the version** (V 143–151, from the MB Ugarit tablet) must stay `[…]` in the `>` lines; give the edition's translation in the apparatus and a clearly marked footnote, and tag every parallel that rests on it as such – otherwise the most quoted lines of a tablet (the auras seized by hand) enter the commentary as if attested.
- **A tool's lemma check can collide with a correct rendering when the edition's lemma is wrong** (V 283 *arrāt* lemmatized *arru*): render to pass the check, record the cognate in the apparatus and the index, and propose the tool fix rather than editing the tool.

## Tablet VIII

### Scope
SB Tablet VIII, all 206 eBL labels in eBL's order (1–47, 47a, 48–79, 84–115, 117–173, 173a, 174–185, 199–219, 229, 230, 250): the dawn (1–2); Gilgameš's lament, in which the paths of the Cedar Forest, the elders and the crowd of Uruk, the mountains, the pastures, the trees, the animals, the rivers Ulāya and Euphrates, the ploughman, the shepherd, the brewer, the harlot and the young men are bidden to weep for Enkidu (3–41); "hear me, young men … like a mourning woman I wail", the weapons and the festive garment that "an evil wind" snatched, the friend as mule, wild donkey and panther, and the deeds in the forest and against the Bull (42–54); "what is this sleep?", the heart that does not beat, the face veiled like a bride's, the eagle, the lioness, the hair torn out and the finery cast off (55–64); the craftsmen and the statue of lapis and gold (65–79); the funeral promises of VII 140–147 in Gilgameš's own mouth (84–91); the treasury, the grave goods "set for his friend", the slaughter, the meat for "the princes of the earth" (92–134); the gifts displayed to Šamaš for each god of the netherworld – Ištar, Bēlet-ilī, Ereškigal, Dumuzi, Namtar, Ḫušbišag, Qāssa-ṭābat, Ninšuluḫḫatumma, Bibbu, Dumuzi-abzu and others – each with "may X receive this, may he/she welcome my friend and walk at his side" (135–207); the damming of the river, the table of *elammakku*-wood, the bowls of honey and butter (208–219); fragments (229–250). Manuscripts: Nineveh NinNA1a–e (V₁–V₅) and NinNA2 (R), Ḫuzirina HuzNA1 (e, Sultantepe; Assyrianizing and corrupt), Babylon BabNB1a–c (m₁, m₂ and the new BM 36999). Most lines rest on one or two manuscripts; 80–83, 116, 186–198, 220–228, 231–249 are lost.

### Sources consulted
- eBL `L/1/4` SB VIII (`../../sources/eBL/Gilgamesh-SB-VIII/`): `reading-edition.md` (N, T, L, the notes quoting George 2003 and Maul 2005, the ATF of every manuscript), `glossary.md` (for the stems and the lemma slips), `manuscripts.md`.
- `../parallels_for.py VIII --all` (62 lines with parallels); the first copies in X (31–33, 53–54, 126–130, 226–230, 259–260, 92/163), VII (140–147), II 174, flood XI 48/97 (the dawn formula), I 98 (*ibtani ina libbīša*).
- `../renderings.md` incl. §9–§16 (binding); both `project.md` files with their tablet sections; the format models (flood XI 1–60, Enūma eliš VI 1–30); `annotated-X.rtl.md` and `annotated-VII.rtl.md` as the nearest models.
- George's translation (eBL T), after drafting; Tchernichovsky 1924 (`../../sources/Ben-Yehuda--Tchernichovsky-1924/full-work--all-tablets.rtl.md`, ll. 2303–2460, his Tablet VIII) after pass (c).
- The vocalized Bible through `../../tools/check_bible_quotes.py --show`.

### Decisions
1. **Generated from one data file per part** (scratchpad `VIII-part1–4.txt` + `gen8.py`, headers in `VIII-head-*.txt`), as for X, II, III and V: the eBL normalization is inserted by script, so the annotated `>` lines and the clean translation are identical by construction.
2. **The lament's verb**: *bakû* + accusative = `בָּכָה אֶת` (Gen 50:3 `וַיִּבְכּוּ אֹתוֹ`): every *libkīka* / *libkûnikku* = `יבכה אותך` / `יבכו אותך` (`תבכה` for the harlot); *bakû eli* = `בכה על` (35, 37, 38); *nubbû* (*nabû* II D, 13, 45) = `יִלֵּל` as VII and II 174 – `קָרָא` stays reserved for *nabû* "to name"; *lallartu* (45) = `סוֹפֶדֶת` (Mic 1:8 `אֶסְפְּדָה וְאֵילִילָה`): not `מְקוֹנֶנֶת` (*damāmu* = `קוֹנֵן`, §5), not `מְיַלֶּלֶת` (the verb of the same line); the root ספד is free (Akk. *sapādu* does not occur in the epic).
3. **The weeping world**: *qerbētu* (13) = `נְאוֹת` (Jer 9:9, Amos 1:2, Joel 1:20; eBL's lemma *qerbu* "centre" is a slip) and *merītu* (6) = `מִרְעֶה` [cognate]; *taskarinnu* (14, restored) = `תְּאַשּׁוּר` (Isa 41:19; 60:13), *šurmēnu* = `שׁוּרְבִּין` (§16); *lulīmu* = `יַחְמוּר` (Deut 14:5; `אַיָּל` is *ayyalu*), *dumāmu* = `*תַּן*` (George's "jackal"); the X 259–260 animal names copied; *serrēmu* (5) = `חֲמוֹרֵי הַבָּר` (`עָרוֹד` is *akkannu*, `פֶּרֶא` is reserved); *Ulāya* = `אוּלַי` (Dan 8:2, the biblical name; **always vocalized**: unvocalized it reads "perhaps"); *qadištu* of a river = `קָדוֹשׁ`; *ikkaru* = `אִיכָּר`; *nāqidu* = `נוֹקֵד`; *kaparru* = `נַעַר־הַצֹּאן`; *sābû* (31, emended) = `בַּעַל־הַמִּסְבָּאָה` (the masculine of X's *sābītu*); *alālu* "work-song" (24) = `הֵידָד` (Jer 25:30; 48:33); *ḫīqu* = `*מֶזֶג*` (Song 7:3); *elû* adj. = `עֶלְיוֹן`.
4. **The friend as weapon and the snatching wind**: *ḫaṣṣinnu* `גַּרְזֶן`, *namṣaru* `חֶרֶב`, *šibbu* `חֲגוֹר` (X), *arītu* = `מָגֵן` (2 Sam 1:21), *tukultu* = `מִבְטָח` (as an earlier tablet's `מבטח אחיו`), *lubār isinnātīya* = `בגד חגיי` (as VI `בגד חגייך`), *nēbeḫ lalêya* `אבנט עונגי`; *šāru lemnu* = `רוח רעה` (both "evil wind" and "demon"); *ekēmu* = `חָטַף` (§10).
5. **Death and mourning**: *šittu* = `שֵׁנָה` (not `תַּרְדֵּמָה`, Gen 2:21, which Tchernichovsky uses); *adāru* N = `התקדרת` (§9); *rēša našû* = `נשא ראש` (Gen 40:13); *nakādu* = `הָלַם` (not `פָּעַם`, Gen 41:8); *katāmu* = `כִּסָּה`; *kallatu* = `כַּלָּה` [cognate] (§15); *sâru* = `כִּרְכֵּר` (§13); *nēštu* = `לְבִיאָה` (no other lioness word; *labbu* = `לָבִיא`); 61 *šuddât* rendered with the variant *ina šuttati* `*בשוחות*` (eBL's lemma "pitfall"; George "deprived of"); *saḫāru* Gtn = `חוזר וחוזר` (§8, §11); *baqāmu* = `מָרַט` (Ezra 9:3); *tabāku* = `יָצַק` (63, 76, 131; never `צָבַר`); *qunnunu* = `מְסֻלְסָל`; *nasāḫu* = `נוֹסֵחַ` (vocalized); *damqūti* = `הנאות` (*damqu* = `נָאֶה`, VII 138); *asakkiš* = `כדבר אסור` (not `תּוֹעֵבָה` / `חֵרֶם`).
6. **The statue**: *ṣalmu* = `צֶלֶם` (§9); *banû* = `בָּנָה` (69, 212, and the doubtful restoration in 4); *minâtu* = `מִדּוֹת` (flood XI 3); the craftsmen *nappāḫu* `נַפָּח`, *kutimmu* `צוֹרֵף`, *qurqurru* `חרש־נחושת`, *kabšarru* `חרש־אבן`, *zadimmu* `[מלטש־אבנים?]`; *uqnû* `לָזוּרִית`, *ḫurāṣu* `זָהָב`, *irtu* `חָזֶה`, *zumru* `גֵּו`.
7. **VIII 84–91 = VII 140–147 in the first person.** The Akkadian of 84–89 is the same as VII (Š present *ušnālka*, *ušeššebka*, *ušabkâkku*, *umallâkka* are both 3rd sg. and 1st sg.), but the speaker here is Gilgameš (George: "I shall lay you out"), so the Hebrew has `אשכיב`, `אושיב`, `אַבְכֶּה`, `אַאֲבִיל`, `אמלא`; 90 changes *šū … pagaršu* to *anāku … pagrī* (`ואני … את גוויתי`); 91 *altabbiš-ma … arappud* = `אלבש … [ומשו]טט אני בשדה` (X 62's `ומשוטט אני בשדה`). Everything else is VII's Hebrew, with this copy's brackets.
8. **The grave goods**: the refrain *ištakan ana ibrīšu* = `שׂם לְרֵעֵהוּ` (*šakānu* reserved; George "provided for"); *KIMIN* counted as preserved; *manû* = `מָנֶה` [cognate] (1 Kgs 10:17); *biltu* = `מַשָּׂא` (§10/§14); *šinni pīri* = `שֵׁן פִּיל`; *išpatu* = `אַשְׁפָּה` [cognate]; *ḫutpalû* = `מַפֵּץ` (Jer 51:20; `אַלָּה` collides with "oath" and "goddess"); *naṣbatu* = `*ידית*` (meaning unknown; George "handle"); *šikru* = `נִצָּב` (Judg 3:22); *katappû* (175) = `*פיפיות*` (George; eBL's "bridle" does not fit); *nikkas* = `חצי קנה` (*nindanu* = `קָנֶה`); *kubru* = `עֹבִי` (vocalized `עוֹבְיָם`: unvocalized "עבים" = clouds); *ṣibittu* = `*אחיזה*`; *parūtu* = `שַׁיִשׁ`; *šikkatu* = `פַּךְ`; *ebbūbu* = `אַבּוּב` [cognate] (`חָלִיל` belongs to *ḫalālu*, §16); *kussû* = `כִּסֵּא`; *werû* = `נְחֹשֶׁת`, *parzillu* = `בַּרְזֶל`; *ṭabāḫu* = `טָבַח` (as flood XI 70); *immeru* = `כֶּבֶשׂ` (as flood XI 71), *alpu* = `שׁוֹר`; *kabru* = `שָׁמֵן` (not `בָּרִיא`, which would echo `וּבְרִיאֹת בָּשָׂר`, Gen 41:2); *marû* = `מְרִיא` [cognate]; *malkū ša qaqqari* = `נסיכי הקרקע` (VII 143).
9. **The netherworld formulas** (135–207): *ana* DN … *šamaš uktallim* = `בעבור DN, …, לְשַׁמַשׁ הֶרְאָה:` (*ana* = `בעבור`, to keep the recipient apart from Šamaš, to whom the gift is shown; *kullumu* = `הֶרְאָה`, vocalized throughout); *limḫur* DN = `יקבל` / `תקבל` (*maḫāru* "receive", in the sense of an offering); *ana pān ibrīya lū ḫadât-ma idāšu lillik* = `לקראת רֵעִי תהי שמחה, ולצדו תלך!` / masc. `יהי שמח … ילך` (203 without *lū*: `שמח`). Šamaš `שַׁמַשׁ` vocalized at every occurrence (unvocalized `לשמש` = "to serve").
10. ***erṣetu* = `אֶרֶץ`, never `שְׁאוֹל`** (no Akkadian word justifies it): `מלכת הארץ`, `שׂר הארץ`, `סוכנת הארץ`, `טבח הארץ`, `הארץ הרחבה` (*erṣetu rapaštu*, George "populous"), with the note at 145 (1 Sam 28:13 `עֹלִים מִן הָאָרֶץ`; Jonah 2:7) and the index rows tagging `שְׁאוֹל` as the biblical equivalent. *mātu* (66) is also `אֶרֶץ` (the living land), noted.
11. **Names** (vocalized at first occurrence): `גִּלְגָּמֶשׁ` (2), `אֶנְקִידוּ` (3), `אוּרוּךְ־הַדִּיר` (9, note `אֶרֶךְ`), `אוּלַי` (18, always), `פְּרָת` (19), `חוּמְבָּבָה` (54), `שַׁמַשׁ` (132, always), `אִשְׁתַּר` (134), `בֵּלֶת־אִילִי` (140), `אֶרֶשְׁכִּיגַל` (145), `דּוּמוּזִי` (149, note `תַּמּוּז`), `נַמְתָּר` (154), `חוּשְׁבִּישָׁה` (159, N *hušbiša*), `קַאסַּה־טָבַת` (164, "her hand is good", transliterated so that `טוֹב` stays for *ṭābu*), `נִינְשׁוּלוּחַתוּמַּה` (171), `בִּבּוּ` (177), `דּוּמוּזִי־אַבְּזוּ` (181), `אֲנוּנָּכִּי` (210); titles: *sukkallu* = `שַׂר` (not `מִשְׁנֶה`, Gen 41:43), *abarakkatu* = `סוֹכֶנֶת` (1 Kgs 1:4), *šābiṭu* = `מְטַאטֵא`, *mušēširat bīti* = `מיישרת הבית` (*ešēru* ~ ישר), *ṭābiḫu* = `טַבָּח` (Gen 37:36), *mašḫaltappû* transliterated `מַשְׁחַלְתַּפּוּ`, *dayyānu* = `דיין`.
12. **The river and the table**: *sikru* (212, = *sekēru*) = `סֶכֶר` [cognate of `וַיִּסָּכְרוּ`, Gen 8:2]; *ibtani ina libbīšu* = `בנה [בלבו]` (as I 98 `בנתה בלבה`); *paššūru* = `שולחן`; *elammakku* = `אֵלַמַּכּוּ` (note on `אַלְמֻגִּים`); *mallatu* = `קְעָרָה`; *dišpu* = `דְּבַשׁ` [cognate]; *ḫimētu* = `חֶמְאָה` [cognate] (30, 217); *zaʾānu* D = `עִטֵּר`.
13. **Words in round brackets in N** (only in some manuscripts) are not translated (VII/X rule): 4 *(u)*, 15 *(ina uzzīni)*, 17 *(u)*, *(ša)*, 38 *(ka)*, 51 *(ibrī)*, 55 *(eninna)*, *(ša)* (but `(אשר)` added for sense), 63/64 *(u)*, 177 *(rapašti)*.
14. **eBL slips recorded in the apparatus**: 13 *qerbētu* as *qerbu* "centre"; 13, 45 the D "lament" under *nabû* "to name"; 6, 17, 91 *ṣēri* "back"; 18, 46 *aḫu* "brother" for *aḫu* "side"; 66 *ultēṣi* as *šasû* (it is *aṣû* Š); 76 *itabbak* as *bakû* (63 correctly *tabāku*); 131 *kabrūti* as *kabrūtu* "thickness"; 168 *parūtu* II "quiver?" against George's "alabaster"; 175 *katappû* "bridle"; eBL's parallels field links 131 to X 231 (unrelated) and 64 to 78 (position only).

### Open decisions for the user
1. **45 *lallartu* = `סוֹפֶדֶת`** (chosen; ספד is free in this corpus) or `מְקוֹנֶנֶת` (Jer 9:16 `לַמְקוֹנְנוֹת`, the closer social match, but קונן is *damāmu*)?
2. **One Hebrew `צד` for two Akkadian words**: *aḫu* "side" (18, 46) and the adverbial *idā-* "at the side of" in the refrain (`ולצדו ילך/תלך`, 138–203); alternatives for the refrain: `ועמו ילך`, `ובזרועו ילך` (literal *idu* = `זרוע`, unidiomatic). (X 257 has *ana lēt* = `אל צד` too.)
3. **61** `כלביאה אשר גוריה *בשוחות*` follows eBL's lemma and the variant; George's "deprived of her cubs" would give `כלביאה ששכלה את גוריה` (2 Sam 17:8 `כְּדֹב שַׁכּוּל`) – a stronger biblical echo, but not what the lemma says.
4. **14 *taskarinnu* = `תְּאַשּׁוּר`**: if the user chooses `תְּאַשּׁוּר` for *šurmēnu* (Tablet V open decision 4), *taskarinnu* needs another word (`אשכרוע`, the botanical boxwood).
5. **The netherworld titles**: *sukkallu* = `שַׂר` (or `שָׁלִיחַ`, Namtar being Ereškigal's messenger); *abarakkatu* = `סוֹכֶנֶת` (1 Kgs 1:4 – the word of Abishag) or the plainer `אם־הבית`; *mušēširat bīti* = `מיישרת הבית` (keeps the cognate ישר) or George's sense `מנקה הבית`.
6. ***kullumu* = `הֶרְאָה`** (13 times) and *ana* DN = `בעבור` (chosen) or George's sense "displayed (before Šamaš) for DN" = `הציג`?
7. **212** `את סכר הנהר בנה [בלבו]` (George 2003 after Cavigneaux: the dam for the tomb in the riverbed) or the older reading "the word/idea of the river" (*zikru*, after I 98)?
8. **The flood corpus's XI 102 `הגליש את הסכרים`** renders *miḫru* "weir" with סכר, the cognate of *sekēru* (VIII 212, Gen 8:2); align it (e.g. `הגליש את המִפְלָשִׂים`/`הסוללות`) so that סכר stays for *sekēru*?

Changes: 56 `שומעני – [אותי]` (only the suffix of *tašemmânni* is broken); 67 `חרש־נחושת` unbracketed (root *qrqr* preserved); 97 `שׂם` unbracketed (*ta-kan* preserved); 123 `הַמַּפֵּץ`; 131 `[שוורים שמנים]` (*kabrūti* mostly broken).

Tchernichovsky 1924 Tablet VIII compared (ll. 2303–2460; he knew only 40–64 and the X refrain): he imports `תַּרְדֵמָה הָעֲמֻקָּה` (Gen 2:21; 15:12) for *šittu*, `פְּקֻדַּת כָּל־הָאָדָם` (Num 16:29), `קְבַרְתִּיו מִלְּפָנָי` (Gen 23:4), `נִמְשַׁל לְעָפָר מִן־הָאֲדָמָה` (Gen 2:7), `הַר־הָאֱלֹהִים`, `לְעוֹלְמֵי־עַד`, `אַהֲבַת־אָח`; none is in the Akkadian, nothing changed. His `קָדַרְתָּ`, `נָגַע אֶל לִבּוֹ וְהוּא לֹא הָלַם` and `כְּכַסּוֹת אֶת הַכַּלָּה` agree with ours independently.

| 17 | nēšu | אריה | `מִנְּשָׁרִים קַלּוּ מֵאֲרָיוֹת גָּבֵרוּ` (שמואל ב א:כג) | [divergence] | David compares the dead to lions and eagles; here lions weep, and Gilgameš is the eagle (60). |

| 18 | nittallaku | התהלכנו | `מִתְהַלֵּךְ בַּגָּן לְרוּחַ הַיּוֹם` (בראשית ג:ח) | [equivalent] | *alāku* Gtn = התהלך (§16). Minor. |
| 19 | elletu purattu | פְּרָת הטהור | `וְהַנָּהָר הָרְבִיעִי הוּא פְרָת` (בראשית ב:יד) | [equivalent] | **Key.** The Euphrates, the fourth river of the garden, here a "pure" river that weeps for the dead (and whose water the friends libated, 20). |
| 19 | purattu | פרת | `הַנָּהָר הַגָּדֹל נְהַר פְּרָת` (בראשית טו:יח) | [equivalent] | Name. |
| 20 | ša nittaqqû mê nādi | אשר נסכנו מי נאדות | `וַיַּסֵּךְ עָלֶיהָ נֶסֶךְ` (בראשית לה:יד) | [equivalent] | Libation; here water libated to Šamaš and Lugalbanda on the road (George). |
| 21 | libkûnikku eṭlūtu ša uruk | יבכו אותך עלמי אורוך | `בְּנוֹת יִשְׂרָאֵל אֶל שָׁאוּל בְּכֶינָה` (שמואל ב א:כד) | [equivalent] | The people of the hero's city weep (young men here, daughters there). |
| 23 | libkīku ikkaru | יבכה אותך האִיכָּר | `הֹבִישׁוּ אִכָּרִים הֵילִילוּ כֹּרְמִים` (יואל א:יא) | [cognate] | **Key.** *ikkaru* / אכר (Sumerian loan through Akkadian); farmers wail. |
| 23 | ikkaru | האיכר | `וְקָרְאוּ אִכָּר אֶל אֵבֶל וּמִסְפֵּד אֶל יוֹדְעֵי נֶהִי` (עמוס ה:טז) | [cognate] | The farmer called to mourn, beside the professional wailers (cf. 45). |

| 47 | arītu ša pānīya | המגן אשר לפניי | `כִּי שָׁם נִגְעַל מָגֵן גִּבּוֹרִים מָגֵן שָׁאוּל` (שמואל ב א:כא) | [equivalent] | **Key.** The shield in David's lament; Enkidu as Gilgameš's shield. |

| 50 | ṭardu | נרדף | `נָע וָנָד תִּהְיֶה בָאָרֶץ` (בראשית ד:יב) | [equivalent] | The hunted, banished one (= X 53). |

| 58 | ilput libbašu ul inakkud | נגע בלבו – ואינו הולם | `וַיָּמָת לִבּוֹ בְּקִרְבּוֹ וְהוּא הָיָה לְאָבֶן` (שמואל א כה:לז) | [equivalent] | The heart that stopped. |
| 58 | ilput | נגע | `וְלֹא תִגְּעוּ בּוֹ פֶּן תְּמֻתוּן` (בראשית ג:ג) | [equivalent] | *lapātu* = נגע (§10); here touch reveals death. Formal. |

| 59 | kīma kallati | ככלה | `וַתִּקַּח הַצָּעִיף וַתִּתְכָּס` (בראשית כד:סה) | [equivalent] | The bride veils herself before the bridegroom. |
| 59 | iktum | כיסה | `וַתִּקַּח מִיכַל אֶת הַתְּרָפִים וַתָּשֶׂם אֶל הַמִּטָּה … וַתְּכַס בַּבָּגֶד` (שמואל א יט:יג) | [equivalent] | An image on a bed, covered, in place of the man (cf. the statue, 69). |
| 59 | pānuš | את פניו | `וְהַמֶּלֶךְ לָאַט אֶת פָּנָיו` (שמואל ב יט:ה) | [divergence] | David covers his own face in mourning for Absalom; Gilgameš covers the dead friend's. |
| 60 | kīma arê isâr elīšu | כנשר מכרכר עליו | `כְּנֶשֶׁר יָעִיר קִנּוֹ עַל גּוֹזָלָיו יְרַחֵף` (דברים לב:יא) | [equivalent] | The eagle hovering over its young. |
| 60, 63 | kīma arê; ibaqqam … pērta | כנשר; מורט … המחלפות | `קָרְחִי וָגֹזִּי … הַרְחִבִי קָרְחָתֵךְ כַּנֶּשֶׁר` (מיכה א:טז) | [equivalent] | Eagle and shorn hair together in a mourning call. |
| 61 | kīma nēšti ša šuddât mērānīša | כלביאה אשר גוריה *בשוחות* | `וַיִּשְׁמְעוּ אֵלָיו גּוֹיִם בְּשַׁחְתָּם נִתְפָּשׂ` (יחזקאל יט:ד) | [equivalent] | **Key.** A lioness whose cub is caught in a pit, in a *qinah* (`מָה אִמְּךָ לְבִיָּא`, יחזקאל יט:ב; `וְאַתָּה שָׂא קִינָה`, יחזקאל יט:א). Our rendering follows eBL's lemma and the variant *ina šuttati*. |
| 61 | kīma nēšti | כלביאה | `כְּדֹב שַׁכּוּל בַּשָּׂדֶה` (שמואל ב יז:ח) | [equivalent] | The bereaved beast – **rests on George's translation** ("deprived of her cubs"), not on the lemma; also `אֶפְגְּשֵׁם כְּדֹב שַׁכּוּל` (הושע יג:ח). |
| 63 | ibaqqam … pērta | מורט … המחלפות | `וָאֶמְרְטָה מִשְּׂעַר רֹאשִׁי וּזְקָנִי` (עזרא ט:ג) | [equivalent] | Tearing out the hair in grief. |
| 63 | ibaqqam | מורט | `לֹא תִתְגֹּדְדוּ וְלֹא תָשִׂימוּ קָרְחָה בֵּין עֵינֵיכֶם לָמֵת` (דברים יד:א) | [divergence] | Mourning rites on the body, forbidden in Deuteronomy; also `וְלֹא יִתְגֹּדַד וְלֹא יִקָּרֵחַ לָהֶם` (ירמיהו טז:ו). |
| 64 | inassaḫ inaddi damqūti | נוסח, משליך את הנאות | `וַיִּקְרַע יַעֲקֹב שִׂמְלֹתָיו וַיָּשֶׂם שַׂק בְּמָתְנָיו וַיִּתְאַבֵּל עַל בְּנוֹ יָמִים רַבִּים` (בראשית לז:לד) | [equivalent] | **Key.** Mourning by the clothes (Jacob tears, Gilgameš tears off). Also `וַיִּקְרַע אֶת בְּגָדָיו וַיִּשְׁכַּב אָרְצָה` (שמואל ב יג:לא). |
| 64 | damqūti | הנאות | `וַיִּתְאַבָּלוּ וְלֹא שָׁתוּ אִישׁ עֶדְיוֹ עָלָיו` (שמות לג:ד) | [equivalent] | Finery removed in mourning; also `וְאֶת בִּגְדֵי רִקְמָתָם יִפְשֹׁטוּ` (יחזקאל כו:טז). |
| 67 | nappāḫu … kutimmu kabšarru | נַפָּח … צורף, חרש־אבן | `הַפֶּסֶל נָסַךְ חָרָשׁ וְצֹרֵף בַּזָּהָב יְרַקְּעֶנּוּ` (ישעיהו מ:יט) | [equivalent] | The craftsmen who make an image (there mocked). |
| 67–69 | epuš ibrī … ṣalam ibrīšu | עשה את רעי … צלם רעהו | `וַיַּעֲשֵׂהוּ כְּתַבְנִית אִישׁ כְּתִפְאֶרֶת אָדָם לָשֶׁבֶת בָּיִת` (ישעיהו מד:יג) | [divergence] | An image "in the form of a man": in Isaiah an idol, here a dead friend's likeness. |
| 69 | ibnâ ṣalam ibrīšu | בנה צלם רעהו | `נַעֲשֶׂה אָדָם בְּצַלְמֵנוּ כִּדְמוּתֵנוּ` (בראשית א:כו) | [cognate] | **Key / required row.** *ṣalmu* / צלם: God makes man in his image; here man makes an image of a dead man. |
| 69 | ṣalam | צלם | `וַיִּבְרָא אֱלֹהִים אֶת הָאָדָם בְּצַלְמוֹ בְּצֶלֶם אֱלֹהִים בָּרָא אֹתוֹ` (בראשית א:כז) | [cognate] | **Key.** As above; cf. I 49 (Gilgameš's form drawn, *eṣēru* + *ṣalmu*) and X 317 (death has no image). |
| 69 | ṣalam ibrīšu | צלם רעהו | `וַיּוֹלֶד בִּדְמוּתוֹ כְּצַלְמוֹ` (בראשית ה:ג) | [cognate] | The image passed from father to son; Enkidu has no son (50, "mule"), so the image is made by hand. |
| 69 | ibnâ ṣalam | בנה צלם | `לֹא תַעֲשֶׂה לְךָ פֶסֶל וְכָל תְּמוּנָה` (שמות כ:ג) | [divergence] | **Key.** The law forbids exactly what Gilgameš commissions. |
| 69 | ibnâ ṣalam | בנה צלם | `אָרוּר הָאִישׁ אֲשֶׁר יַעֲשֶׂה פֶסֶל וּמַסֵּכָה תּוֹעֲבַת יְהוָה מַעֲשֵׂה יְדֵי חָרָשׁ` (דברים כז:טו) | [divergence] | The craftsman's image cursed. |
| 69 | ṣalam ibrīšu | צלם רעהו | `כִּי אָמַר אֵין לִי בֵן בַּעֲבוּר הַזְכִּיר שְׁמִי וַיִּקְרָא לַמַּצֶּבֶת עַל שְׁמוֹ` (שמואל ב יח:יח) | [equivalent] | A monument for the memory of a man without a son. |
| 69 | ibnâ | בנה | `וַיִּבֶן יְהוָה אֱלֹהִים אֶת הַצֵּלָע` (בראשית ב:כב) | [equivalent] | *banû* = בנה: the verb of Enkidu's making (I 101) is the verb of his image. |
| 71 | īnīka ša uqnî iratka ša ḫurāṣi zumurka | עיני⟨ך⟩ של לזורית, חזך של זהב, גווך | `הוּא צַלְמָא רֵאשֵׁהּ דִּי דְהַב טָב חֲדוֹהִי וּדְרָעוֹהִי דִּי כְסַף` (דניאל ב:לב) | [equivalent] | **Key.** A *ṣalmu* / צלמא described limb by limb in precious materials, the chest (*irtu* / חדוהי) among them. |

| 215 | paššūr elammaki | שולחן של אֵלַמַּכּוּ | `וְגַם אֳנִי חִירָם … הֵבִיא מֵאֹפִיר עֲצֵי אַלְמֻגִּים הַרְבֵּה מְאֹד` (מלכים א י:יא) | [cognate] | *elammakku* ~ אלמגים / אלגומים (`וְאַלְגּוּמִּים מֵהַלְּבָנוֹן`, דברי הימים ב ב:ז); a precious wood from Syria / Lebanon. The equation is a common scholarly proposal, not certain. |
| 215 | paššūr … šamaš uktallim | שולחן … לשמש הראה | `הַעֹרְכִים לַגַּד שֻׁלְחָן וְהַמְמַלְאִים לַמְנִי מִמְסָךְ` (ישעיהו סה:יא) | [equivalent] | A table set for a god (Gad, Meni); here for the sun-god and the dead. |
| 216 | umalli dišpa | מילא דבש | `חֶמְאָה וּדְבַשׁ יֹאכֵל` (ישעיהו ז:טו) | [cognate] | **Key.** *dišpu* / דבש and *ḫimētu* / חמאה (217) – the same pair. |

### Lines shared with other tablets
- **The dawn formula** 1, 65, 92, 213 = flood XI 48, 97 (`כשהאיר אור הבוקר,`); 1 has *ana namāri* for *ina namāri* (same sense, same Hebrew, noted); 65 brackets `[הבוקר]` by its manuscripts.
- **X 53–54, 31–33 (§3.2 and the deeds)**: VIII 50 = X 53/126/226 (identical; only X 126's brackets differ); 51 ~ X 54 without `רֵעִי` (*ibrī* in round brackets in N: only NinNA2) and with the dash that leads into the relative clause; 52 = X 31/128/228; 53 `אחזנו, ושור השמים [קטלנו],` – the Akkadian has *alâ* once, after the first verb (*niṣbatū-ma alâ nināru*), against X 129 *alâ niṣbatū-ma alâ nināru*: the Hebrew order follows; 54 ends with a full stop (X goes on to the lions, VIII does not) and brackets `[יושב]`.
- **X 259–260**: 16–17 reuse the animal names (`דב, צבוע, נמר, *ברדלס*`; `אַיָּל, יָעֵל, בהמה, הַנָּעִים של השדה`) as a list without article; new here `יחמור`, `*תן*`, `אריה, רְאֵם`.
- **VII 140–147** → 84–91 (first copy in VII): identical words, first person instead of third (84–86, 88–89; the Akkadian forms are ambiguous between 1st and 3rd sg., the context decides), 90 *anāku … pagrī* (`ואני … גוויתי`), 91 `אלבש … ומשוטט אני` (Gt perfect and present 1st sg.; VII 147 3rd sg.); 87 identical (brackets only). verify.py lists 84–86, 88–90 as differences: all justified so.
- **X 92, 163, XI 287** → 211 `[גילגמ]ש – כש[מעו] את זאת,` (brackets only).
- **II 174** `במרירות מְיַלֵּל?` ~ 45 `איילל במרירות` (the same *ṣarpiš unabba*, 1st sg.).
- **Inside the tablet**: 63 = 76 (76 has *u* as a written word, 63 only in some manuscripts: `ויוצק` / `, יוצק`; brackets), 64 = 77 (brackets), 69 ~ 72 ~ 74, 71 ~ 73 (2nd → 3rd person), 170 = 185; the refrains 97–126 and 136–203 (identical per gender). verify.py's "64 ≠ 78" (78 is unreadable) and "X 231 ≠ 131" are false eBL links.

### Proposals for the shared renderings
1. **Key terms (§4)**: *erṣetu* = `אֶרֶץ` confirmed for the netherworld formulas (`מלכת הארץ`, `הארץ הרחבה` for *erṣetu rapaštu*), with `שְׁאוֹל` only in the apparatus and the index (XII will need the same); *ṣalmu* = `צֶלֶם` (VIII 69, 72, 74) added to the Gen 1:26–27 rows with Exod 20:3, Deut 27:15 [divergence]; *dišpu* = `דְּבַשׁ` [cognate] beside *ḫimētu* = `חֶמְאָה`; *sikru* / *sekēru* = `סֶכֶר` / `סָכַר` [cognate ↔ Gen 8:2] (reserve סכר for *sekēru*; open decision 8 on flood XI 102); *kallatu* = `כַּלָּה` confirmed (VIII 59); *ikkaru* = `אִיכָּר` ↔ Joel 1:11, Amos 5:16; *qerbetu* = `נְאוֹת` and *merītu* = `מִרְעֶה`; *lallartu* = `סוֹפֶדֶת` (open decision 1); *nubbû* = `יִלֵּל`; *šittu* = `שֵׁנָה` (never `תַּרְדֵּמָה`); *manû* = `מָנֶה`; *išpatu* = `אַשְׁפָּה`; *marû* = `מְרִיא`; *ṭabāḫu* = `טָבַח`, *ṭābiḫu* = `טַבָּח`; *ebbūbu* = `אַבּוּב`; *alālu* = `הֵידָד`.
2. **Names (§1)**: Ulāya = `אוּלַי` (**always vocalized**, add to §7); Ḫušbišag = `חוּשְׁבִּישָׁה`; Qāssa-ṭābat = `קַאסַּה־טָבַת`; Ninšuluḫḫatumma = `נִינְשׁוּלוּחַתוּמַּה`; Bibbu = `בִּבּוּ`; Dumuzi-abzu = `דּוּמוּזִי־אַבְּזוּ`; netherworld titles *sukkallu* `שַׂר`, *abarakkatu* `סוֹכֶנֶת`, *šābiṭu* `מְטַאטֵא`, *ṭābiḫu* `טַבָּח`, *mašḫaltappû* `מַשְׁחַלְתַּפּוּ`, *mušēširat bīti* `מיישרת הבית`.
3. **Formulas (§2)**: *ištakan ana ibrīšu* = `שׂם לְרֵעֵהוּ`; *ana* DN … *šamaš uktallim* = `בעבור DN, …, לְשַׁמַשׁ הֶרְאָה:`; *limḫur* DN = `יקבל` / `תקבל` DN; *ana pān ibrīya lū ḫadât-ma / ḫadī-ma idāšu lillik* = `לקראת רֵעִי תהי שמחה, ולצדו תלך!` / `יהי שמח … ילך!`; *libkīka* / *libkûnikku* = `יבכה אותך` / `יבכו אותך`.
4. **Ordinary words (§5)**: *ḫalāpu* I "slip through" = `חָמַק`; *serrēmu* = `חֲמוֹר בָּר`; *lulīmu* = `יַחְמוּר`; *dumāmu* = `*תַּן*`; *taskarinnu* = `תְּאַשּׁוּר` (open decision 4); *kaparru* = `נַעַר־הַצֹּאן`; *sābû* = `בַּעַל־הַמִּסְבָּאָה`; *ḫīqu* = `*מֶזֶג*`; *elû* (adj.) = `עֶלְיוֹן`; *tukultu* = `מִבְטָח`; *arītu* = `מָגֵן`; *isinnu* = `חַג`; *mukillu* = `מַחֲזִיק`; *nakādu* = `הָלַם`; *katāmu* = `כִּסָּה`; *nēštu* = `לְבִיאָה`; *baqāmu* = `מָרַט`; *qunnunu* = `מְסֻלְסָל`; *asakkiš* = `כדבר אסור`; *nappāḫu*, *kutimmu*, *qurqurru*, *kabšarru*, *zadimmu* (decision 6); *riksu* = `קֶשֶׁר`; *šukuttu* = `עֲדָיִים`; *parūtu* = `שַׁיִשׁ`; *kubru* = `עֹבִי`; *ḫutpalû* = `מַפֵּץ`; *naṣbatu* = `*ידית*`; *šikru* = `נִצָּב`; *nikkas* = `חֲצִי קָנֶה`; *ṣibittu* = `*אחיזה*`; *kussû* = `כִּסֵּא`; *werû* = `נְחֹשֶׁת`; *parzillu* = `בַּרְזֶל`; *šemeru* = `צָמִיד`; *šikkatu* = `פַּךְ`; *mešēltu* = `מַשְׁחֶזֶת`; *mallatu* = `קְעָרָה`; *paššūru* = `שֻׁלְחָן`; *kabru* = `שָׁמֵן`; *immeru* = `כֶּבֶשׂ`; *maḫāru* "receive" = `קִבֵּל`; *dayyānu* = `דַּיָּן` (plene `דיין`).
5. **§7 "always vocalize"**: `אוּלַי` (the river / "perhaps"), `לְשַׁמַשׁ` (the god / "to serve"), `נוֹסֵחַ` (tears out / version), `הֶרְאָה`, `עוֹבְיָם` ("clouds"), `שַׁיִשׁ` ("six"), `הַמְטַאטֵא` (sweeper / broom).
6. **verify.py**: nothing needed for VIII.

### Lessons for the guidelines
- **A lament list is a parallels list.** Where a source bids nature and people to mourn one by one (VIII 3–41), render each item by its own word and look up each in the prophetic laments (Jer 9, Joel 1, Amos 1, Zech 11, Lam 1) as well as in 2 Sam 1: the hits are lexical (נאות, אכר, ברוש/ארז, צבי, אח/אחות), not only thematic.
- **Ambiguous verb forms that serve two persons** (Akkadian Š/D present: 1st sg. = 3rd sg. *u-*) make a "repeated" line differ in Hebrew: render by the speaker, and justify each verify.py difference by the person, not by the letters.
- **A formula that the netherworld shares with the Bible's Sheol (`אֶרֶץ`) must be rendered by the cognate, and the biblical name (`שְׁאוֹל`) kept for the index**: otherwise either the parallel disappears or a Hebrew word without an Akkadian counterpart enters the text.

## Tablet IX

### Scope
SB Tablet IX, all 137 eBL labels in eBL's order (1–29, 37–59, 74–93, 125–176, 184–196): Gilgameš weeps for Enkidu and roams the wild in fear of death (1–5); the road to Ūta-napišti, the lions in the mountain passes, the prayer to Sîn, the dream and the fight with the lions (6–29); Mount Māšu, which guards the sun's rising, its tops at the "base of heaven", its breast reaching *arallû*; the scorpion-man and his female at its gate (37–59); Gilgameš states his quest (75–77); the scorpion-man describes the twelve *bēru* of darkness inside the mountain (78–93), Gilgameš insists (125–128), the scorpion-man blesses his going and opens the gate (129–137); the road of the sun: twelve *bēru*, each with the refrain "dense was the darkness, and light was there none; it did not let him look behind him" (138–170); the trees of the gods bearing carnelian, lapis lazuli and other stones, "thorn and briar" of gems (171–194); Gilgameš walking about, the ale-wife watching him (195–196). Manuscripts: Nineveh NinNA1 (K 2360 = George D) for almost the whole tablet, NinNA2 (JJ) for 1–12, NinNA3 (EE) for 142–148: one witness for most lines, so readings rather than variants. 30–36, 60–73, 94–124, 177–183 and 197–end are lost.

### Sources consulted
- eBL `L/1/4` SB IX (`../../sources/eBL/Gilgamesh-SB-IX/`): `reading-edition.md` (N, T, L, the notes quoting George 2003, the ATF), `glossary.md` for doubtful lemmas, `manuscripts.md`.
- `../parallels_for.py IX --all`; the first copies in X (62/139/239, 93–96, 164–165, 124/217/224, 92/163, 79, 7, 28), I 48, flood XI 7 and XI 23 (Ubār-Tutu), VII 206 (the raised head), VIII 211.
- `../renderings.md` incl. §9–§17; both `project.md` files with their tablet sections (X and VIII in full); the format models (flood XI 1–60; X as nearest model).
- George's translation (eBL T) after drafting; Tchernichovsky 1924 Tablet IX (`../../sources/Ben-Yehuda--Tchernichovsky-1924/full-work--all-tablets.rtl.md`, ll. 2461–2826) after pass (c).
- The vocalized Bible through `../../tools/check_bible_quotes.py --show`.

### Decisions
1. **Generated from one data file per tablet** (scratchpad `IX.dat` + `gen.py`, eBL's N inserted by script): the annotated `>` lines and the clean translation are identical by construction.
2. **The darkness refrain** follows the Akkadian, not the expected formula: *šapât ekletum-ma ul ibašši nūru* = `עֲבָה החשכה, ואין אור;` (*šapû* II "be thick, dense", stative → adjective `עֲבָה`, vocalized; *ekletu* = `חֲשֵׁכָה`, *nūru* = `אוֹר`); *ul inaddinšum-ma ana palāsi arkassu* = `אינה נותנת לו להסתכל לאחוריו.` (*palāsu* = `הִסְתַּכֵּל`, *arkatu* "rear" = `אחוריו`, as V 136, 164, X 91). First copy 83 (the scorpion-man's speech; a nominal clause fits both his description and the narrative) and 141; copied letter for letter in 140–166 with each copy's brackets. The SB text has "behind him" only: there is no "ahead" (Tchernichovsky's `מִפָּנִים וּמֵאָחוֹר` adds it). 83 has N *ibbašši*, the others *ibašši*: same Hebrew.
3. ***bēru* = `בֵּרוּ`** (always vocalized, masculine numerals: `בֵּרוּ אחד`, `שני בֵּרוּ`, `שלושה` … `שנים־עשר`); the numeral line keeps eBL's order (number first). *lām šamši* (170) = `קודם לשמש` (`טֶרֶם` barred; `לפני השמש` reads spatially).
4. **The jewel trees**: *iṣṣī ša ilī* (172) = `עצי האלים` – no `גן`, no `עדן` (no *kirû*); *iṣu* = `עֵץ` (172 only). *sāmtu* = `אוֹדֶם` (written plene with niqqud: `אֹדֶם` strips to "אדם"); *uqnû* = `לָזוּרִית` (not `סַפִּיר`, apparatus only); *inbu* = `תְּנוּבָה`; *isḫunnatu* = `אשכולות`; *alālu* D stative = `מְתוּלֶּה`; *ana dagāli ḫīpat* = `לצפייה – *מזוקק*` (*ḫiāpu* "to purify", George "lovely"); *ḫasḫaltu* = `עלווה`; *ana amāri ṣayyaḫ* = `למראה – עֲרֵבָה` (*ṣayyaḫu* = `עָרֵב`, §13; `נֶחְמָד` barred); *baltu u ašāgu* = `חוח וחדק` (§11); *ḫarūbu* = `חרוב` [cognate]; *zinû* = `גבעול`; *išqillatu* = `צדפה`; uncertain stones transliterated, vocalized: `פַּפַּרְדִּלּוּ` (agate?), `*לָרוּשׁ*` (coral?), `סָסוּ`, `אַנְזַגּוּלְמֶה`, `אַבַּשְׁמוּ`, `שׁוּבּוּ`, `שָׁדָנוּ` (haematite), `אֲגוּסִיגוּ` (turquoise?).
5. **The mountain**: *šad māši* = `הר מאשו` (note on *māšu* "twin"); *elû* "top" = `פסגותיהם` (not `ראשם`: no *rēšu*, so Gen 11:4 / 28:12 `וְרֹאשׁוֹ` stays in the apparatus); *šupuk šamê* = `מסד השמים` (`יסוד` is taken by I 1, 3, 19); *emēdu* stative = `סמוכות` (§13); *arallû* = `אֲרַלּוּ` (transliterated; `שְׁאוֹל` only tagged); *irtu* = `חזה`; *kašādu* = `מגיע` (↔ Gen 28:12 `מַגִּיעַ`).
6. **The scorpion-people**: *girtablilu* = `אִישׁ־הָעַקְרָב`, pl. `אַנְשֵׁי־הָעַקְרָב`, his *sinništu* = `אשתו` (§1 `אֵשֶׁת־הָעַקְרָב`); *naṣāru* = `נוצרים` (↔ Gen 3:24, tagged; `הַכְּרֻבִים` only in the apparatus); *rašbat pulḫassunu* = `נוראה אימתם` (*rašbu* = `נורא` §9; *pulḫu* = `אימה`); *imru* = `מבט`; *galtu* = `איום` (new; keeps `נורא` for *rašbu*); *melammū* = `זהרם`; *saḫāpu* = `סוחף` [cognate סחף]; *puluḫtu u rašubbatu* = `אימה ומורא` (*rašubbatu* = `מורא`, new; ↔ Gen 9:2); *arāmu* "cover" = `עטה` (`כסה` is *katāmu*); *zikaru* = `הזכר` (§10).
7. ***karābu* = `בֵּרֵךְ`** in the prayer to Sîn (10, 24: `את סִין מברך`), as VII 79 `אברך את אָנוּ`, although George has "praying"; *supû* = `שוועות` (new; `תחנונים`/`תחינה` are taken).
8. **Repeated lines (binding)**: 5 = X 62 exactly; 15 = X 93 (brackets); 16 `שלף [חרב] חגורו,` – construct *namṣār šibbīšu* without *ina* (George 2003), against X 94 `מחגורו`; 17 = X 96; 51 = I 48 exactly – 49 and 51 (the scorpion pair's exchange) are therefore given without quotation marks; 76 follows flood XI 7 (`עמד באספת האלים, וחיים ביקש`, *šeʾû* = `בִּקֵּשׁ` though George has "found"); 77 `מוות וחיים` as X 321; 78–79 the two-line speech formula (§2); 126 = X 217 in the first person without negation; 136 = X 92.
9. ***urḫa ṣabātu* / *ḫarrāna ṣabātu* = `אחז דרך` / `אחז אורח`** (7, 138), the idiom of Job 17:9 `וְיֹאחֵז צַדִּיק דַּרְכּוֹ`; *ḫarrān šamaš* (138) = `אורח שַׁמַשׁ` because N normalizes the divine name there (39, 45, 84–86, 170 have *šamšu* = `הַשֶּׁמֶשׁ`).
10. **Other words**: *ṣarpiš* = `במרירות`; *nērebētu ša šadî* = `מבואות ההרים` (X 34); *parāru* D = `פורר` [cognate]; *eṣēru* D = `צִייֵּר`; *nawirtu* = `נהרה` (11, 25, 171); *namirtu šaknat* = `נהרה שׂוּמָה`; *ebēršina pašqu* = `אשר צר לעבור בהן` (`מעבר` is *nēberu*); *tānēḫu* = `אנחה`; *zikru* = `מאמר`; *šalmiš* = `בשלום`; *abul šadî* = `שער ההר`; *qitrub* = `קרוב`; *iltānu* = `רוח הצפון`; *alāku* Gtn = `בהתהלכו` (195, §16); *šuātu* = `בו, בו` (196, as VII 206).
11. **eBL slips recorded**: *ṣēru* lemmatized "back" (2, 5, 191); *šitta* (142) lemmatized *šittu* "sleep" (it is "two"); *balti* (188) lemmatized *bāštu* "dignity" (it is *baltu* "thorn"); the eBL parallels 146 ≈ 156 and X 138 ≈ IX 5 are false links (verify.py lists them).

### Open decisions for the user
1. **The refrain's adjective**: `עֲבָה החשכה` (chosen: *šapû* "thick, dense", as the Akkadian) or `כבדה החשכה` / `סמיכה החשכה`? And `לאחוריו` for *arkassu* (shared with *kutallu* `אחוריים` in III) or `לאחור`?
2. **172 `עצי האלים`**: no `גן` (no *kirû*). If the user wants the reading translation to name the place, a heading `## עצי האלים` is used, not "גן".
3. **174 *ḫīpat* = `*מזוקק*`** (lemma *ḫiāpu* "purify") or George's sense "lovely" (`*נאה*`)?
4. **10, 24 *karābu* = `מברך`** (VII's rendering) or by sense `מתפלל` (George "praying")?
5. **40 *šupku* = `מסד`** (chosen) or `יסוד` (shared with *išdu*/*temennu* in I)?
6. **173 `אוֹדֶם` with a written vav** (to pass the strip test) vs the biblical `אֹדֶם`: a general rule for vocalized words whose defective form collides (`אדם`).

### Progress log
- 2026-10-17/18: read the brief, the whole guidelines, corpus.md, renderings.md (incl. §9–§17), both project files with their sections (X, VIII in full), the format models; ran `parallels_for.py IX --all`; read the whole of eBL SB IX (N, T, L, notes, ATF); grepped the new Hebrew strings against the earlier `>` lines (`עבה` only V 43, X 97 for *šapû*; `חשכה` VII 184; `נהרה` OB VA+BM 13′–14′, consistent).
- Drafted all 137 entries in 10 sections in `IX.dat` and generated `annotated-IX.rtl.md` and `translation-IX.rtl.md` (6 footnotes).
- Pass (a) fidelity, against N and the ATF in a compact N | Hebrew listing: the refrain's brackets recomputed per copy (143, 152 `החשכה` unbracketed: *ek-l[e-* keeps the root; 144, 150, 162, 166 `נותנת` bracketed: *i-na-a[n-*, *i-nam#-[*, *i-na[m-*; 142, 148 `בֵּרוּ` unbracketed: *DANN[A*); 78 `[ואמר]`; 12 quotation marks closed; 189 punctuation.
- Pass (b) consistency: verify.py (repeated lines against X, I 48, flood XI); `מסד` replacing `יסוד` (collision with I 1, 3, 19); `שוועותיי` plene instead of a vocalized defective form; strip test on every vocalized word (`צִייֵּר`, `מְתוּלֶּה`, `אוֹדֶם` corrected).
- Pass (c) Hebrew: read aloud; the refrain kept short and identical; Tchernichovsky 1924 Tablet IX compared after drafting: he imports `גַּן־הָאֱלֹהִים`, `פִּרְיוֹ`, `נֶחְמָד וְטוֹב לָעֵינָיִם`, `תַּאֲוָה לָעֵינָיִם`, `עֵץ־הַחַיִּים`, `כַּקּוֹץ וְכַדַּרְדַּר`, `רְקִיעַ־הַשָּׁמָיִם`, `שְׁאוֹל־תַּחְתִּית`, `עָפָר מִן־הָאֲדָמָה`, `וַיְהִי אוֹר`, `שׁוֹמְרִים`, and "ahead" in the refrain – none of them in the Akkadian; nothing changed. His `מַר יִבְכֶּה`, `נְקִיקֵי הֶהָרִים`, `אָיֹם`, `מָוֶת בְּמַבַּט־עֵינֵיהֶם`, `אַשְׁכֹּלוֹת … תְּלוּיִים` agree with ours independently.
- `python3 -I verify.py IX`: 137 entries = eBL 137, 137 translation lines, **problems 0**; 3 differences in repeated lines (justified below) and the bracket-only differences of the refrain. `check_bible_quotes.py` on both files: 0 problems (two fixed: a reference without book name, a maqaf quotation of 1 Kgs 7:9).

| 172 | iṣṣī ša ilī | עצי האלים | `וַיְקַנְאֻהוּ כָּל עֲצֵי עֵדֶן אֲשֶׁר בְּגַן הָאֱלֹהִים` (יחזקאל לא:ט) | [equivalent] | **Key.** "Trees of the gods" ↔ "trees of Eden in the garden of God"; also `כָּל עֵץ בְּגַן אֱלֹהִים לֹא דָמָה אֵלָיו בְּיָפְיוֹ` (יחזקאל לא:ח). No *kirû* ("garden") in the Akkadian. |
| 172 | iṣṣī ša ilī | עצי האלים | `וַיַּצְמַח יְהוָה אֱלֹהִים מִן הָאֲדָמָה כָּל עֵץ נֶחְמָד לְמַרְאֶה וְטוֹב לְמַאֲכָל וְעֵץ הַחַיִּים בְּתוֹךְ הַגָּן` (בראשית ב:ט) | [divergence] | **Key / required row.** Divine trees at the eastern edge of the world; *iṣu* = עץ [cognate]; but no garden, no tree of life: the trees are of stone. |
| 173 | sāmtu našât inibša | אודם – נושא את תנובתו | `אֹדֶם פִּטְדָה וְיָהֲלֹם` (יחזקאל כח:יג) | [equivalent] | **Key / required row.** *sāmtu* "the red (stone)" = אודם "the red": both named by the colour, different roots; the first stone in Ezekiel's list and the breastplate (`טוּר אֹדֶם פִּטְדָה וּבָרֶקֶת הַטּוּר הָאֶחָד`, שמות כח:יז). |
| 173 | inibša | תנובתו | `וַתִּקַּח מִפִּרְיוֹ וַתֹּאכַל` (בראשית ג:ו) | [divergence] | **Key.** "Fruit" that is not to be eaten: *inbu* = תנובה, never פרי (§10). |
| 172–194 | (the jewel trees) | עצי האלים … אודם … לזורית | `בְּעֵדֶן גַּן אֱלֹהִים הָיִיתָ כָּל אֶבֶן יְקָרָה מְסֻכָתֶךָ` (יחזקאל כח:יג) | [equivalent] | **Key / required row.** Precious stones in the place of God; Ezekiel's list: אֹדֶם (*sāmtu* 173), סַפִּיר (~ *uqnû* 175?), שֹׁהַם; the Eden word is Ezekiel's, not the Akkadian's. |
| 172–194 | (stones in the far land) | אבנים | `וּזֲהַב הָאָרֶץ הַהִוא טוֹב שָׁם הַבְּדֹלַח וְאֶבֶן הַשֹּׁהַם` (בראשית ב:יב) | [equivalent] | **Key / required row.** Precious stones at the edge of the world near the garden; no Akkadian stone here is the cognate of `שֹׁהַם` or `בְּדֹלַח`. |
| 174 | isḫunnata ullulat | באשכולות מתולה | `הִבְשִׁילוּ אַשְׁכְּלֹתֶיהָ עֲנָבִים` (בראשית מ:י) | [equivalent] | Grape clusters (of stone). |
| 174, 176 | ana dagāli … / ana amāri ṣayyaḫ | לצפייה … / ולמראה – עֲרֵבָה | `כָּל עֵץ נֶחְמָד לְמַרְאֶה וְטוֹב לְמַאֲכָל` (בראשית ב:ט) | [equivalent] | **Key / required row.** *ana amāri ṣayyaḫ* "delightful to see" ≈ `נֶחְמָד לְמַרְאֶה` almost word for word (`נחמד` barred, *ṣayyaḫu* = ערב); [divergence] no `וְטוֹב לְמַאֲכָל`. |
| 176 | ana amāri ṣayyaḫ | ולמראה – עֲרֵבָה | `וְכִי תַאֲוָה הוּא לָעֵינַיִם` (בראשית ג:ו) | [equivalent] | The tree pleasing to the eyes. |
| 175 | uqnû | לזורית | `כְּמַרְאֵה אֶבֶן סַפִּיר דְּמוּת כִּסֵּא` (יחזקאל א:כו) | [equivalent] | The Bible's blue stone `סַפִּיר` is probably lapis lazuli; no lexical link, not used in the Hebrew. Also `כְּמַעֲשֵׂה לִבְנַת הַסַּפִּיר` (שמות כד:י). |
| 185 | erēnu | ארז | `אֲרָזִים לֹא עֲמָמֻהוּ בְּגַן אֱלֹהִים` (יחזקאל לא:ח) | [cognate] | Cedar among the trees of the gods. |
| 186 | pappardilû | פפרדלו | `וְהַטּוּר הַשְּׁלִישִׁי לֶשֶׁם שְׁבוֹ וְאַחְלָמָה` (שמות כח:יט) | [equivalent] | If *pappardilû* is agate, its biblical counterpart may be `שְׁבוֹ` (LXX ἀχάτης). Uncertain identification on both sides. |
| 188 | kīma balti u ašāgi … AN.ZA.GUL.ME | כחוח וחדק … אנזגולמה | `וְקוֹץ וְדַרְדַּר תַּצְמִיחַ לָךְ` (בראשית ג:יח) | [divergence] | **Key.** Instead of thorns and thistles, gems; *baltu u ašāgu* = חוח וחדק (§11), not קוץ ודרדר. Verb restored. |
| 188 | kīma balti … | כחוח … | `תַּחַת הַנַּעֲצוּץ יַעֲלֶה בְרוֹשׁ` (ישעיהו נה:יג) | [equivalent] | **Key.** In place of the thornbush something precious grows. |
| 188 | balti | חוח | `חוֹחַ עָלָה בְיַד-שִׁכּוֹר` (משלי כו:ט) | [equivalent] | The word. |
| 189 | ḫarūba ilput | בחרוב נגע | `וְלֹא תִגְּעוּ בּוֹ פֶּן תְּמֻתוּן` (בראשית ג:ג) | [divergence] | Touching a tree: permitted here, the tree is stone. Weak; *ilput* partly restored. *ḫarūbu* / חרוב [cognate] (Mishnaic). |
| 195 | itallukīšu | בהתהלכו | `מִתְהַלֵּךְ בַּגָּן לְרוּחַ הַיּוֹם` (בראשית ג:ח) | [equivalent] | **Key.** *alāku* Gtn = התהלך (§16): walking about among the trees – a man, not God, and no garden. |
| 195 | itallukīšu | בהתהלכו | `בְּתוֹךְ אַבְנֵי אֵשׁ הִתְהַלָּכְתָּ` (יחזקאל כח:יד) | [equivalent] | **Key.** Walking among precious stones in the chapter of `בְּעֵדֶן גַּן אֱלֹהִים` (יחזקאל כח:יג). |

### Lines shared with other tablets
- **X (first copies, binding)**: 5 = X 62, 139, 239 (identical); 15 = X 93, 164 (brackets); 16 ~ X 94, 165 – construct *namṣār šibbīšu* without *ina* (George 2003): `שלף [חרב] חגורו,` vs `שלף חרב מחגורו,` – justified by the Akkadian; 17 = X 96 (brackets); 126 ~ X 124, 217, 224 – first person (`פניי`) without *lā*, so `צרובים פניי` without `לא` (verify lists IX 126 ≠ X 124: justified); 136 = X 92, 163 (brackets); 80 ~ X 79 (`"לא היה, גילגמש,` + `כ[מוך]`); 75 ~ X 73 (`אורח אוּתְנַפִּשְׁתִּי`); 77 ~ X 321 (`מוות וחיים`); 49 ~ X 7 (`בשר אֵלִים`).
- **I 48** → 51 identical; therefore 49 and 51 without quotation marks.
- **Flood XI 7** → 76 (third person, relative clause: `עמד … ביקש`); XI 23 → 6 (`בן אוּבָּר־טוּטוּ`).
- **VII 206** → 196 (`נשאה [את ראשה, מ]ביטה בו, בו.`, the emphatic pronoun).
- **Inside the tablet**: 10 ~ 24, 11 ~ 25, 12 ~ 26 (first/third person); 53 = 130; 78 = 129; 85 = 86; the refrain (83, 140–165; 141–166) identical except brackets.
- **False eBL links** listed by verify.py: IX 146 ≈ 156 (a refrain line against the other refrain line) and X 138 ≈ IX 5 (*ādur* vs *mūta aplaḫ*).

### Proposals for the shared renderings
1. **Fixed passage (§3)**: the IX refrain `עֲבָה החשכה, ואין אור;` / `אינה נותנת לו להסתכל לאחוריו.`; the *bēru* numerals (`בֵּרוּ אחד`, `שני בֵּרוּ` … `שנים־עשר בֵּרוּ`).
2. **Names (§1)**: `הַר מָאשׁוּ` (used), `אֲרַלּוּ` (*arallû*, transliterated, `שְׁאוֹל` only tagged), `אַנְשֵׁי־הָעַקְרָב` (pl.); stones: `פַּפַּרְדִּלּוּ`, `*לָרוּשׁ*`, `סָסוּ`, `אַנְזַגּוּלְמֶה`, `אַבַּשְׁמוּ`, `שׁוּבּוּ`, `שָׁדָנוּ`, `אֲגוּסִיגוּ`.
3. **Key terms (§4)**: *šapû* II (darkness) = `עָבָה`; *sāmtu* = `אוֹדֶם` (plene with niqqud); *isḫunnatu* = `אֶשְׁכּוֹל`; *ḫasḫaltu* = `עלווה`; *ḫarūbu* = `חָרוּב` [cognate]; *rašubbatu* = `מוֹרָא`; *galtu* = `אָיֹם`; *saḫāpu* = `סָחַף` [cognate]; *arāmu* "cover" = `עָטָה`; *imru* = `מַבָּט`; *supû* = `שַׁוְעָה`; *šupku* = `מַסָּד`; *elû* "top" = `פסגה`; *girtablilu* + *naṣāru* ↔ Gen 3:24 confirmed as the corpus's main guardian row; *urḫa/ḫarrāna ṣabātu* = `אחז דרך/אורח` (Job 17:9).
4. **Ordinary words (§5)**: *parāru* D = `פורר`; *tānēḫu* = `אנחה`; *zinû* = `גבעול`; *išqillatu* = `צדפה`; *lām* = `קודם ל־`; *šalmiš* = `בשלום`; *abul šadî* = `שער ההר`; *iltānu* = `רוח הצפון`; *ḫiāpu* stative = `*מזוקק*`; *eṣēru* D = `צִייֵּר`.
5. **§7 "always vocalize"**: `עֲבָה` (thick, fem.), `עֲרֵבָה` (pleasant / steppe / willow), `אוֹדֶם` (carnelian / man), `צִייֵּר`.

### Lessons for the guidelines
- **A refrain that a coordinator expects may not be the one the edition has**: the brief's "dense was the darkness … behind him or ahead" corresponds to *šapât ekletu … arkassu* (behind only). Fix a refrain from the edition's first preserved copy, not from a summary or a translation.
- **The strip test catches name–word collisions in vocalized Hebrew** (`אֹדֶם` strips to "אדם"): for such words write the vav (`אוֹדֶם`), and list the case in the renderings.

## Tablet XII

### Scope
SB Tablet XII, all 139 eBL labels in eBL's order (1–37, 37a, 38–119, 129–137, 144–153): Gilgameš's lament for the *pukku* and *mekkû* fallen into the netherworld (1–5); Enkidu offers to bring them up (6–9); Gilgameš's instructions – the taboos of the netherworld (10–30); Enkidu goes down and does every forbidden thing, and "the earth seized him" (31–54); Gilgameš weeps before Enlil, Sîn and Ea (55–79); Ea speaks to Šamaš, who opens a hole in the earth and brings up Enkidu's ghost "like a breath" (80–87); the embrace and the question "tell me the order of the earth that you saw" (88–95); Enkidu's body eaten and filled with dust (96–101); the fates of the dead: the fathers of one to seven sons (102–116), the palace eunuch? (117–119), fragments (129–137), the man struck by a mooring-pole, the one who died his god's death, the one killed in battle, the one whose corpse lies in the open country, the one whose ghost has no caretaker (144–153). The tablet is an Akkadian translation of the second half of the Sumerian *Bilgameš, Enkidu and the Netherworld* (BN c. 172–303), known here only through eBL's notes (George 2003). Manuscripts: Nineveh NinNA1–4 (N, HH, G₁–₂, U₁–₂), AssaNA1 (KK), Babylon BabNB1–2 (a, q), and line 1 as the catch-line of XI (NinNAQuo1–2). 120–128 and 138–143 are lost; the end is missing.

### Sources consulted
- eBL `L/1/4` SB XII (`../../sources/eBL/Gilgamesh-SB-XII/`): `reading-edition.md` (N, T, L, the notes quoting George 2003 / RN117, Maul 2005 / RN602 and RN2813xs on the Sumerian recensions), `manuscripts.md`. ETCSL 1.8.1.4 is not in `sources/` and was not used.
- `../parallels_for.py XII --all` (no line repeats another tablet; 71 internal parallels); `../renderings.md` §4 (*eṭemmu*, *utukku*, *zaqīqu*, *takkapu*, *erṣetu*), §9 (*pukku*, *mekkû*), §17 (VIII's netherworld decisions); VII 184–209 and VIII 135–207 (netherworld vocabulary); flood XI 64 (*sikkatu* = `יתד`), XI 102 (*tarkullu* = `עמוד הקשירה`), XI 161 (`הריח`).
- George's translation after drafting; Tchernichovsky 1924 Tablet XII (ll. 4119–4510) after pass (c).

### Decisions
1. **Generated from one data file** (scratchpad `XII.dat` + `gen.py`), as IX.
2. ***erṣetu* = `הָאָרֶץ`** throughout (never `שְׁאוֹל`; §17); `אל הארץ` keeps the ambiguity of the Akkadian and the Sumerian (*kur*: "to the ground" = "to the netherworld"). The netherworld formula *erṣetu iṣbassu* = `הארץ אֲחָזַתְהוּ` (*ṣabātu* = `אחז`, suffixed, vocalized), *ul iṣbassu* = `לא אֲחָזוֹ` (vocalized: unvocalized "אחזו" reads "they held"), *iṣabbatka* = `תֹּאחֲזְךָ` (as §3.1 `תֹּאחֲזֵהוּ`).
3. **Command and execution** (13–30 → 33–50): every prohibition is *lā* + present = `לא` + future (the apodictic form of the Decalogue, not `אל`); the execution repeats the words in the past and third person (`לא תלבש` → `לבש`, `יתקבצו אליך` → `התקבצו אליו`, `יסובבו אותך` → `סבבו אותו`); the brackets follow each copy. The narrative adds 37a (`האִטִּים נזדעזעו;`) and has *ina* where the command has *ana* (39, 42).
4. **The taboo words**: *ṣubātu zakû* = `שׂמלה זכה`; *ubāru* = `גֵּר` (new) and *wadû/edû* D = `ידע` (`יֵדְעוּךָ`, `יָדְעוּהוּ`; eBL lemma *wadû*); *šaman pūri ṭābu* = `שמן צלוחית טוב`, *pašāšu* = `סך`; *pūru* = `צלוחית` (`קערה` is *mallatu*); *erīšu* = `ריח`; *tilpānu* = `מקל־זריקה` (CDA "bow", George "throwstick"); *lamû* = `סובב`; *šabbiṭu* = `שבט` [cognate, new]; *arāru* III "tremble" = `נזדעזע` (false friend of *arāru* I, reserved ארר); *šēnu* / *šênu* = `נעליים … תִּנְעַל` (vocalized: *edēlu* = `נָעַל` "lock"); *rigma šakānu* = `קול … תָּשִׂים`; *râmu* = `אהב`, *zêru* = `שנא`, *našāqu* = `נשק`, *maḫāṣu* = `מחץ`; *tazzimtu* = `תלונה`; *ṣalālu* = `יָשֵׁן` (`הַיְּשֵׁנָה`, vocalized); *būdu* = `כתף`; *šadādu* = `משך` (12, 32 `להוראתי [תִּמָּשֵׁךְ]` / `לא נמשך`; 30, 50 `לא/אינו משוך (בבגד)`); *aširtu* = `הוראה` (not `תורה`).
5. **The demons and gods**: Namtar `נַמְתָּר`, Asakku `אֲסַכּוּ`; *rābiṣ Nergal* = `רוֹבֵץ נֵרְגַל` – the participle of *rabāṣu* (§11 `רבץ`), so that the [cognate] with Gen 4:7 `רֹבֵץ` is visible (George "sheriff"); *lā pādû* = `אשר אינו חומל`; Ninazu `נִינַאזוּ`; Ekur `אֵכּוּר`; Ur = `אוּר` (the biblical name, Gen 11:28); Eridu `אֶרִידוּ`; Ningal `נִינְגַל`; *qarrādu eṭlu Šamaš* = `הגיבור, העלם, שַׁמַשׁ`. **The god who opens the hole is Šamaš** (eBL, line 85 *šamaš mār Ningal*; Sumerian Utu), not Nergal as in older translations and in Tchernichovsky.
6. **The ghost**: *utukku* = `רוּחַ` (§4, noted at 87; *utukku* is a near-synonym of *eṭemmu*, so a different word from `אִט`), *zaqīqu* = `כְּמַשַּׁב` (not `הֶבֶל`; not `כמשב רוח`, since `רוח` is already the ghost), *takkapu* = `נקב`, *elû* Š = `הֶעֱלָה` / `אעלה לך` (8–9, 59, 83, 87; ↔ 1 Sam 28:11, 13, 15), *eṭemmu* = `אִט` (always vocalized; `אִטּוֹ`, `לאִטּוֹ`).
7. **The meeting**: *edēru* N = `לָפְתוּ (זה את זה)` (§13), *našāqu* Dt = `התנשקו`; *malāku* Gt = `נמלכו`; *šâlu* Gtn = `שאלו ושאלו` (cognate of `שֹׁאֵל`, Deut 18:11; no `שוב ושוב`); *ûrti erṣeti* = `צו הארץ` (*uʾʾurtu* = `צו`, §15).
8. **The body**: *išaru* = `אבר`, *ūru* = `ערוה` (reserved), *lapātu* = `נגע`; *gušūru labīru* = `קורה נושנה` (`ישן` is *ṣalālu*); *kalmatu* = `עש` (George 2003: the clothes-moth grub); *nigiṣṣu* = `נקיק`; *eperu* = `עפר` (99–101); *napalsuḫu* = `התפלש` (the biblical mourning verb of Jer 6:26, Ezek 27:30; tagged [equivalent], the sound-likeness noted, no cognate claimed).
9. **The fates**: the dialogue formula *ša … tāmur / ātamar* = `"את אשר … – ראית?" "ראיתי:`; *mārūšu ištēn-ma* = `אשר לו בן אחד` (by sense); *sikkatu* = `יתד` (flood XI 64 binds; VII 87 `יתד` is *deššu*: a collision recorded); *retû* = `תקועה` (of a peg); *marṣiš* = `בכאב`; *takšû* = `אמתחת`; *ṣimdu* = `צמד` [cognate]; *ṭupšarru* = `סופר`, *issu petât* = `זרועו פתוחה`; *išariš* = `ישר`; *errēšu* = `אריס`; *duppussû* = `זוטר`; *šurīnu* = `נֵס`; *aḫiz* = `*נשען*` (George; `אחז` is *ṣabātu*); *tarkullu* = `עמוד הקשירה` (flood XI 102); *lūman* = `לו` (82) / `אוי` (145, George "Alas"); *nasīḫ sikkāti* = `בעקירת היתדות`; *alāku* Gtn = `מתהלך`; *mūt ilīšu imūtu* = `מות אֵלוֹ מת` (vocalized); *mayyālu* = `משכב`; *rēšu našû* = `את ראשו נושאים` (Gen 40:13); *šalamtu* = `נבלה` (`גוויה` is *pagru*); *nadû* stative = `מושלכת` / `מושלכים`; *pāqidu* = `פוקד` [cognate פקד]; *šukkultu* = `גרידות`; *diqāru* = `סיר`; *kusīpu* = `פתיתים` (from *kasāpu* = `פתת`, §2).
10. **Words in round brackets in N are not translated**: 5 *(ʾa)*, 64/72 *(ēdiššīšu)*, 149 *(šu)*; 86 *<<lūman>>* (a scribal error, deleted by eBL).
11. **eBL slips recorded**: the parallels field of 78 points to itself; 51 ≈ 55 is a false link; 75 normalizes *erṣeti* where the formula has *erṣetu*; 89's Sumerian equation *kuš₂.u₃* = *malāku* is an ancient error (George).

### Open decisions for the user
1. **The god of the hole (80–87)**: eBL reads Šamaš (`שַׁמַשׁ`); the brief and older translations have Nergal. Followed eBL; confirm.
2. ***rābiṣu* = `רוֹבֵץ`** (chosen, keeps the cognate of Gen 4:7) or a functional noun (`אורב`, `פקיד`; George "sheriff")?
3. ***zaqīqu* = `כְּמַשַּׁב`** alone (chosen) or `כְּמַשַּׁב רוּחַ` as `../renderings.md` §4 suggests (but `רוח` already renders *utukku*)?
4. ***napalsuḫu* = `התפלש`** (chosen, the biblical mourning verb) or neutral `השתטח`?
5. ***kalmatu* = `עש`** (by George's sense; Isa 51:8, Job 13:28) or literal `כִּנִּים`?
6. ***šabbiṭu* = `שבט`** [cognate] (chosen) or `מטה` / `מקל` (to keep `שבט` out of the Hebrew; it creates the echo with Ps 23:4 and Prov 13:24, which is real but associative)?
7. ***sikkatu* = `יתד`** (flood XI 64) collides with *deššu* = `יתד` (VII 87): give *deššu* another word (`יתד־דלת`?) or *sikkatu* `מסמר`?
8. ***lūman*: `לו` (82) and `אוי` (145)** – one Akkadian word, two Hebrew words, by George's senses. Alternatively `לו` in both (`לו לאמו ולאביו` does not read).
9. **The order of 102 ff.**: our formula `"את אשר לו בן אחד – ראית?" "ראיתי:` or George's "Did you see the man with one son?" without the dash.

- Pass (a) fidelity: brackets recomputed against all manuscripts of each line (e.g. 44, 46, 52–54, 69–70 with *KIMIN* counted as preserved, 100 `בעפר` unbracketed by NinNA3a *e]p-ri*); 3 quotation mark corrected (1–5 is one speech); 41 `[נָעַל]` vocalized; 96, 98 `את` added before the object.
- Pass (b) consistency: the refrains 52–54/60–62/68–70/76–78 and the command/execution pairs compared line by line; `הַמֵּכּוּ` vocalized uniformly; strip test on every vocalized word.

| 91 (93) | ûrti erṣeti ša tāmuru | את צו הארץ אשר ראית | `אֶרֶץ עֵפָתָה כְּמוֹ אֹפֶל צַלְמָוֶת וְלֹא סְדָרִים` (איוב י:כב) | [divergence] | **Key.** Job's netherworld "without order"; here it has an "order", which Enkidu describes. |

| 97 | kī gušūri labīri kalmatu ikkal | כקורה נושנה – עש אוכל | `וְהוּא כְּרָקָב יִבְלֶה כְּבֶגֶד אֲכָלוֹ עָשׁ` (איוב יג:כח) | [equivalent] | **Key.** The body eaten like an old thing; also `כִּי כַבֶּגֶד יֹאכְלֵם עָשׁ` (ישעיהו נא:ח). The Akkadian has "beam", not "garment"; `עש` rests on George 2003's interpretation of *kalmatu*. |
| 97, 99 | kalmatu … epera mali | עש … עפר מלא | `לָבַשׁ בְּשָׂרִי רִמָּה` (איוב ז:ה) | [equivalent] | **Key / required row.** Vermin and dust on the body (Job 7:5 continues `וְגוּשׁ עָפָר`). |
| 99 | epera mali | עפר מלא | `כִּי עָפָר אַתָּה וְאֶל עָפָר תָּשׁוּב` (בראשית ג:יט) | [cognate] | **Key / required row.** *eperu* / עפר: the dead body filled with dust. |
| 99 | epera mali | עפר מלא | `יַחַד עַל-עָפָר יִשְׁכָּבוּ וְרִמָּה תְּכַסֶּה עֲלֵיהֶם` (איוב כא:כו) | [cognate] | Dust and worm over the dead; also `וְלַעֲפַר-מָוֶת תִּשְׁפְּתֵנִי` (תהלים כב:טז). |
| 98 | ūra … talputū | את הערוה אשר נגעת בה | `וְלֹא תִגְּעוּ בּוֹ פֶּן תְּמֻתוּן` (בראשית ג:ג) | [divergence] | Touch and the loss of sexuality in death; *lapātu* = נגע, *ūru* = ערוה (reserved). Associative. |
| 100–101 | ina epri ittapalsiḫ | בעפר התפלש | `וְהִתְפַּלְּשִׁי בָאֵפֶר אֵבֶל יָחִיד עֲשִׂי לָךְ` (ירמיהו ו:כו) | [equivalent] | **Key.** Rolling in the dust as a mourning rite; `התפלש` chosen for *napalsuḫu* (sound-likeness, no cognate claim); also `בָּאֵפֶר יִתְפַּלָּשׁוּ` (יחזקאל כז:ל), `וַיַּעֲלוּ עָפָר עַל רֹאשָׁם` (יהושע ז:ו). |

The ranking of the dead by their sons has no biblical counterpart [absent]. |

| 146 | ša mūt ilīšu imūtu | את אשר מות אלו מת | `תָּמֹת נַפְשִׁי מוֹת יְשָׁרִים וּתְהִי אַחֲרִיתִי כָּמֹהוּ` (במדבר כג:י) | [equivalent] | **Key.** The same figure (verb + internal object "die the death of X"), a good death, and the question of what comes after. |
| 146 | mūt ilīšu | מות אלו | `אִם כְּמוֹת כָּל הָאָדָם יְמֻתוּן אֵלֶּה וּפְקֻדַּת כָּל הָאָדָם יִפָּקֵד עֲלֵיהֶם` (במדבר טז:כט) | [equivalent] | The natural death "of every man". |
| 147 | ina mayyāl ilī ṣalil | על משכב האלים ישן | `יָבוֹא שָׁלוֹם יָנוּחוּ עַל מִשְׁכְּבוֹתָם` (ישעיהו נז:ב) | [equivalent] | **Key.** The good dead lie at rest on their beds. *ilī* restored. |
| 147 | ṣalil | ישן | `כִּי-עַתָּה שָׁכַבְתִּי וְאֶשְׁקוֹט יָשַׁנְתִּי אָז יָנוּחַ לִי` (איוב ג:יג) | [equivalent] | The dead "sleep"; *ṣalālu* = ישן. |
| 147 | mê zakûti išatti | ומים זכים שותה | `עַל-מֵי מְנֻחוֹת יְנַהֲלֵנִי` (תהלים כג:ב) | [equivalent] | Good water. Minor. |
| 148 | ša ina tāḫāzi dīku | את אשר במלחמה נהרג | `אֲשֶׁר יָרְדוּ שְׁאוֹל בִּכְלֵי מִלְחַמְתָּם וַיִּתְּנוּ אֶת חַרְבוֹתָם תַּחַת רָאשֵׁיהֶם` (יחזקאל לב:כז) | [equivalent] | **Key.** The honoured place of the fallen warriors in the netherworld. |
| 149 | rēssu našû | את ראשו נושאים | `יִשָּׂא פַרְעֹה אֶת רֹאשֶׁךָ` (בראשית מ:יג) | [cognate] | **Key.** *rēša našû* = נשא ראש: honouring (the Sumerian has a negation, George 2003). |
| 149 | aššassu ina muḫḫīšu ibakkâ | ואשתו עליו בוכה | `וַיָּבֹא אַבְרָהָם לִסְפֹּד לְשָׂרָה וְלִבְכֹּתָהּ` (בראשית כג:ב) | [equivalent] | Mourning by the spouse. Verb restored. |
| 150 | ša šalamtašu ina ṣēri nadât | את אשר נבלתו בשדה מושלכת | `וְנִבְלָתוֹ תִּהְיֶה מֻשְׁלֶכֶת לַחֹרֶב בַּיּוֹם וְלַקֶּרַח בַּלָּיְלָה` (ירמיהו לו:ל) | [equivalent] | **Key / required row.** The same phrase: corpse + "thrown" (*nadû* = השליך); the fate of an accursed king. |
| 150 | šalamtašu ina ṣēri | נבלתו בשדה | `וְהָיְתָה נִבְלָתְךָ לְמַאֲכָל לְכָל עוֹף הַשָּׁמַיִם וּלְבֶהֱמַת הָאָרֶץ וְאֵין מַחֲרִיד` (דברים כח:כו) | [equivalent] | **Key / required row.** The unburied corpse as curse; also `וְהָיְתָה נִבְלָתָם לְמַאֲכָל לְעוֹף הַשָּׁמַיִם` (ירמיהו טז:ד). *šalamtu* = נבלה. |
| 150 | ina ṣēri | בשדה | `וְאֶת חַיַּת הַשָּׂדֶה לָיְלָה` (שמואל ב כא:י) | [equivalent] | **Required row.** Rizpah guards the corpses in the field; *ṣēru* = שדה. |
| 150–151 | šalamtašu … nadât / eṭemmašu … ul ṣalil | נבלתו … מושלכת / אטו … אינו ישן | `כָּל מַלְכֵי גוֹיִם כֻּלָּם שָׁכְבוּ בְכָבוֹד אִישׁ בְּבֵיתוֹ וְאַתָּה הָשְׁלַכְתָּ מִקִּבְרְךָ כְּנֵצֶר נִתְעָב` (ישעיהו יד:יח) | [equivalent] | **Key / required row.** The kings "lie" in honour, the unburied is "thrown out" (*nadû* = השליך) – and here: "his ghost does not sleep". |
| 151 | eṭemmašu ina erṣeti ul ṣalil | אטו בארץ אינו ישן | `לָמָּה הִרְגַּזְתַּנִי לְהַעֲלוֹת אֹתִי` (שמואל א כח:טו) | [equivalent] | The rest (or disturbance) of the dead. *eṭemmu* = אִט, *ṣalālu* = ישן. |
| 152 | ša eṭemmašu pāqida lā īšû | את אשר אין לאטו פוקד | `וְאֵין מְקַבֵּר לָהֵמָּה הֵמָּה נְשֵׁיהֶם וּבְנֵיהֶם וּבְנֹתֵיהֶם` (ירמיהו יד:טז) | [equivalent] | **Key.** The dead with no one to care for him; *pāqidu* = פוקד (*paqādu* / פקד [cognate]). |
| 153 | kusīpāt akali ša ina sūqi nadâ ikkal | פתיתי לחם אשר ברחוב מושלכים – אוכל | `יִהְיוּ מֻשְׁלָכִים בְּחֻצוֹת יְרוּשָׁלִַם מִפְּנֵי הָרָעָב וְהַחֶרֶב` (ירמיהו יד:טז) | [equivalent] | **Key / required row.** "Thrown in the streets" (*nadû*, *sūqu*); in Jeremiah the corpses, here the scraps the ghost eats. |
| 153 | šukkulāt diqāri … ikkal | גרידות סיר … אוכל | `וְלֹא נָתַתִּי מִמֶּנּוּ לְמֵת` (דברים כו:יד) | [divergence] | **Key / required row.** Food for the dead: disowned in the tithe confession, a necessity here (the ghost without a *pāqidu* eats scraps). |
| 153 | kusīpāt akali | פתיתי לחם | `פָּתוֹת אֹתָהּ פִּתִּים` (ויקרא ב:ו) | [equivalent] | *kusīpu* from *kasāpu* = פתת (§2). |
| 153 | šukkulāt … ikkal | גרידות … אוכל | `שִׁבְעִים מְלָכִים … הָיוּ מְלַקְּטִים תַּחַת שֻׁלְחָנִי` (שופטים א:ז) | [equivalent] | Leftovers as the food of the humiliated. |

### Lines shared with other tablets
- None of XII's lines repeats a line of another tablet (it translates a separate Sumerian poem). Vocabulary shared with VII–VIII and the flood corpus: *erṣetu* = `הארץ` (VIII 145), *sikkatu* = `יתד` (flood XI 64), *tarkullu* = `עמוד הקשירה` (flood XI 102), *eperu* = `עפר` (VII 188–193), *pukku* = `פּוּכּוּ` (I 64, 81).
- **Inside the tablet** (verify.py lists them): 4 ≈ 57 ≈ 65 ≈ 73 and 5 ≈ 58 ≈ 66 ≈ 74 differ only by the vocative (`"אבי אנליל, …`), *-ma*/end-of-speech punctuation and brackets; 54 ≈ 62 ≈ 70 ≈ 78 by the closing quotation mark (62, 70, 78 end the speech) and brackets; 56 ≈ 64 ≈ 72 by the names and the round-bracketed *ēdiššīšu*; 51 ≈ 55 is a false eBL link. The command lines 13–30 and the execution lines 33–50 differ by tense and person exactly as the Akkadian.

### Proposals for the shared renderings
1. **Formulas (§2)**: *erṣetu iṣbassu* = `הארץ אֲחָזַתְהוּ`; *ul iṣbassu* = `לא אֲחָזוֹ`; *ana erṣeti imqutanni* = `אל הארץ נפל לי`; *ultu erṣeti šūlû* = `העלה מן הארץ` (`אעלה לך`); the dialogue formula *ša … tāmur / ātamar* = `"את אשר … – ראית?" "ראיתי:`; prohibitive *lā* + present = `לא` + future (all corpora).
2. **Names (§1)**: Asakku `אֲסַכּוּ`, Ninazu `נִינַאזוּ`, Ekur `אֵכּוּר`, Eridu `אֶרִידוּ`, Ningal `נִינְגַל`, Ur `אוּר` (biblical), *rābiṣu* `רוֹבֵץ` (common noun used as a title).
3. **Key terms (§4)**: *ubāru* = `גֵּר`; *šabbiṭu* = `שֵׁבֶט` [cognate]; *šalamtu* = `נְבֵלָה`; *pāqidu* = `פּוֹקֵד` [cognate]; *napalsuḫu* = `הִתְפַּלֵּשׁ` [equivalent]; *arāru* III = `נִזְדַּעְזַע` (false friend of ARR); *šâlu* = `שָׁאַל` [cognate]; *uʾʾurtu* of the netherworld = `צו הארץ`; *zaqīqu* = `מַשַּׁב` without `רוח` when *utukku* is in the line; *utukku* = `רוּחַ` confirmed; *mekkû* always vocalized `מֵכּוּ`.
4. **Ordinary words (§5)**: *pūru* = `צלוחית`; *tilpānu* = `מקל־זריקה`; *šênu* = `נעל` (vocalized); *lamû* = `סובב`; *būdu* = `כתף`; *aširtu* = `הוראה`; *gušūru* = `קורה`; *labīru* (objects) = `נושן`; *kalmatu* = `עש`; *nigiṣṣu* = `נקיק`; *takšû* = `אמתחת`; *ṣimdu* = `צמד`; *duppussû* = `זוטר`; *šurīnu* = `נֵס`; *tubqu* = `פינה`; *šukkultu* = `גרידות`; *diqāru* = `סיר`; *kusīpu* = `פתיתים`; *marṣiš* = `בכאב`; *išariš* = `ישר`; *išaru* = `אבר`; *ālittu* = `יולדת`.
5. **§7 "always vocalize"**: `אֲחָזוֹ` (him / they held), `הַיְּשֵׁנָה` (asleep / old), `הֶעֱלָה` (brought up / the leaf), `תַּעֲלֶה`, `תִּנְעַל`, `אֵלוֹ` (his god / these), `כְּנֵס`, `בְּכֵה`, `תֵּשֵׁב`.
6. **Collision to resolve**: *sikkatu* (flood XI 64, XII 103, 145) and *deššu* (VII 87) both `יתד` (open decision 7).

### Lessons for the guidelines
- **A biblical idiom can be the exact rendering of a ventive with a dative suffix**: *ušellâkka* = `אַעֲלֶה לָּךְ` (1 Sam 28:11). Translate the ventive-dative literally where Hebrew has the same form, and the parallel appears without any added word.
- **In command-and-execution passages, generate the execution from the command by changing only person and tense**, and let the brackets differ copy by copy; then the script's differences are all of the "justified by the Akkadian" kind.
- **Older translations can carry a wrong actor into a key parallel** (Nergal for Šamaš in XII 80–87, still in Tchernichovsky and many popular versions): check the actor of every key line against the current edition before writing the index row.

## Tablet IV

### Scope
SB Tablet IV, all 175 eBL labels: 1–55, 69–75, 78–109, a+1′–a+9′, 120–142, 155–183, b+1′–b+7′, 210–219, c+1′–c+2′, d+1′. The tablet is one cycle repeated five times (journey formula, well, climb and flour offering "mountain, bring me a dream", Enkidu's "house of the dream-wind", the sleep, the waking questions, the dream, Enkidu's favourable interpretation); the cycles begin at 1, 34, 79, 120, 163 (a+3′–a+5′ is another copy of the formula, place unknown). Preserved dream contents: dream 1 (23–25, the mountain that fell) and dream 3 (101–106, the storm, fire and "death raining"); of dreams 2, 4, 5 only fragments of the interpretation (69–75, 155–162, b+1′–b+7′); 210–219 (NinNA3 = S, placement provisional) a dialogue on the way to the forest. Eleven manuscripts (NinNA1a–c, NinNA2, NinNA3, BabNB1–3, UrkNB1a–b, BabaNB1); most lines in one manuscript; 1–21, 44–55, 78–83, 130–142, 177–183 have no manuscript at all and are restored by eBL from the other copies (fully bracketed here).

### Sources consulted
- eBL `L/1/4` SB IV (`../../sources/eBL/Gilgamesh-SB-IV/reading-edition.md`: N, T, L, parallels, ATF; `manuscripts.md`). eBL has no line notes for IV.
- eBL OB Schøyen₂ (`../../sources/eBL/Gilgamesh-OB-Schoeyen2/reading-edition.md`, N and T) for the OB dreams on the road (not translated in the corpus; cited in the apparatus).
- `../renderings.md` incl. §9–§19; the Hebrew of X 171, XI 301–302, 319–320, III 173, II 193, II 216, V (formulas, *tušāru*, *tumru*, *napāḫu*), VIII (*šittu*), XII (*zaqīqu*, *šalamtu*), OB II/III/VA+BM (*damqu*, *naqû*, *ṣeḫru*), flood XI 1–208 (*mūtu … izannun* = `נִמְטָר`; *târu ana* = `נהפך ל־`) and XI 236 (*šittu irḫâ* = `נִתְּכָה`).
- `../parallels_for.py IV --all`.
- George's translation (eBL T) after drafting; Tchernichovsky 1924 (Tablet IV is inside his "לוח חמישי", ll. 1575–1700 of `../../sources/Ben-Yehuda--Tchernichovsky-1924/full-work--all-tablets.rtl.md`) after pass (c).
- The vocalized Bible through `../../tools/check_bible_quotes.py --show`; the commentary file `../../../../פירוש/1-בראשית/1410-…-סולם_יעקב.rtl.md` (Gen 28:10–22, verses only so far).

### Decisions
1. **Generated from one data file** (scratchpad `iv_data1.py`, `iv_data2.py`, `iv_build.py`): the Hebrew of every repeated block is held once (the canonical strings) and each copy is written with its own brackets; the normalization and George's T are filled in from eBL by script, so the annotated `>` lines and the clean translation are identical by construction.
2. **The fixed Hebrew of the cycle** (first copy 1–33; fixed on the best-preserved copy: 38, 88–100, 92–96):
   `לעשרים בֵּרוּ פָּתְתוּ פַּת,` / `לשלושים בֵּרוּ שָׂמוּ מלון.` (= XI 301–302, with XI 302's full stop: line 3 begins a new sentence) / `חמישים בֵּרוּ הלכו כל היום,` / `מהלך ירח וחצי – ביום השלישי ניגשו אל הר הלבנון.` (the head fixed in X 171; *ṭeḫû* = `נִגַּשׁ`, §9) / `לפני שַׁמַשׁ כָּרוּ בור,` / `מים? … שָׂמוּ ב…` / `עלה גילגמש על קדקד ההר,` / `את קמח־המַצְחַתּוּ שלו ניסך לרמה:` / `"הר, הָבֵא לי חלום – דבר שַׁמַשׁ נאה אראה!"` / `עשה לו אנקידו, לו – בית־מַשַּׁב,` / `דלת־סערה קבע בשערו.` / `השכיב אותו במעגל … הציור,` / `והוא – כמכמורת … שכב בשערו.` / `גילגמש – על כרעיו סמך את סנטרו,` / `השינה הניתכת על האנשים – עליו נפלה.` / `באמצע (הלילה) כילה את שנתו,` / `קם, ושָׂח אל רֵעֵהוּ:` / `"רֵעִי, הלא צעקת אליי? למה אני ער?` / `הלא נגעת בי? למה אני מבולבל?` / `הלא אֵל חלף? למה קָהֶה בשרי?` / `רֵעִי, ראיתי חלום אחד,` (`שני`, `שלישי`, `רביעי`, `חמישי` – the ordinal is the only change, as in the Akkadian) / `והחלום אשר ראיתי – מבולבל כולו.` / and the close of the interpretation `ובבוקר – דבר שַׁמַשׁ נאה נראה."` (33 = 78 = 162), its opening `"רֵעִי, נאה חלומך, יפה …` (28 ~ 109 ~ 155).
3. ***zaqīqu* = `מַשַּׁב`**, `בית־מַשַּׁב` (10, 43, 88, 130, 171), the common noun of §4 and XII (`כְּמַשַּׁב`), not the transliterated god (George: "*house* of Zaqīqu"): the manuscripts write *za-qi₂-qi₂* without the divine determinative, eBL lemmatizes the noun *zīqīqu* "wind", and `מַשַּׁב` keeps the link with Enkidu's ghost in XII. The god of dreams is named in the apparatus (open decision 1).
4. ***šittu* = `שֵׁנָה`** (never `תַּרְדֵּמָה`, §17), and ***reḫû* = `נִתַּךְ`** as XI 236 (`נִתְּכָה עליי השינה`): *šittu rēḫât nišī* = `השינה הניתכת על האנשים`; *maqātu* = `נָפַל`. Job 4:13 / 33:15 `בִּנְפֹל תַּרְדֵּמָה עַל-אֲנָשִׁים` stays in the apparatus.
5. ***šasû* = `צָעַק`** in the waking question (`הלא צעקת אליי?`) – `קָרָא` is reserved for *nabû* (§6); 1 Sam 3:4–8 (`הִנְנִי כִּי קָרָאתָ לִי`) is tagged [equivalent], and Tchernichovsky's `הַאַתָּה קָרָאתָ לִי` is the false echo we avoid. Also in 101 `צעקו השמים`.
6. ***etēqu* = `חָלַף`** (§12) gives `הלא אֵל חלף?` – the verb of Job 4:15 `וְרוּחַ עַל-פָּנַי יַחֲלֹף` without being chosen for it; *šīru* = `בָּשָׂר` (`בשרי`); ***ḫamû* = `קָהֶה`** (new; vocalized): `קפא` is *šuḫarruru* (102), and `רדום` would bring in the root of `תַּרְדֵּמָה`.
7. ***šāšû* = `מְבֻלְבָּל`** (plene `מבולבל`), one word for the man (19) and the dream (22): rejected `נָבוֹךְ` (not for a dream), `נִסְעָר` (beside `סְעָרָה`, 11), `מְשֻׁבָּשׁ` (not for a person).
8. ***qablītu* = `אמצע (הלילה)`** (with *qabaltu* = `אֶמְצַע`): **not `אַשְׁמֹרֶת`**, the exact biblical term (Judg 7:19 `הָאַשְׁמֹרֶת הַתִּיכוֹנָה`), because it carries the root שמר, kept out of the `>` lines (§6, Gen 3:24).
9. ***maḫāru* Š *šuttam* = `מְרַצֶּה לו את חלומו`** (27, 108; new): Enkidu "makes the dream acceptable to him". The SB tablet has no *pašāru* in its preserved lines (the OB Schøyen₂ parallels have *šuttam ipaššar*): `פָּשַׁר` stays reserved for *pašāru*, and `פָּתַר` is nowhere. The interpretation's verdict *damqat šunatka* = `נאה חלומך` (*damqu* = `נָאֶה`, OB II; `טוֹב` is *ṭābu*).
10. **Other new words**: *ḫarāru* = `כָּרָה` (§10) and *būru* = `בּוֹר`; *muḫḫu* = `קדקד` also in *ina muḫḫi* (as V `קדקדו`; `ראש` is *qaqqadu*/*rēšu*); *maṣḫatu* = `קמח־המַצְחַתּוּ` (transliterated; `סֹלֶת` would import Lev 2); *naqû* = `נִסֵּךְ` (§19); *kippatu* = `מַעְגָּל`; *uṣurtu* = `צִיּוּר` (with *eṣēru* D = `צִייֵּר`); *šēšû* = `מִכְמֹרֶת` (`רֶשֶׁת` is *pūgu*, OB II); *šarbillu* = `סְעָרָה` (open decision 3); *kinṣu* = `כְּרָעַיִם` (`שׁוֹק` is *purīdu*, `בֶּרֶךְ` is *birku*); *leʾû* = `יָכֹל`; *mitluku* (*malāku* Gt) = `להימלך`; *ramāmu* = `רָעַשׁ` (`רָעַם` is *ragāmu*, V); *barāqu* / *birqu* = `בָּרַק` / `בָּרָק` [cognate]; *napāḫu* N = `נִפַּח` (V *napāḫu* = `נָפַח`); *nablu* = `שַׁלְהֶבֶת` (`להב` is *mešēltu*, OB III); *šapû* II Gtn = `התעבו פעם אחר פעם` (IX *šapû* = `עָבָה`; §11 no `שוב ושוב`); *daʾāmu* = `דָּעַךְ`; *nebû* = `בּוֹהֵק` (`זֹהַר`, `נֹגַהּ` are taken); *belû* = `כָּבָה`; *qatnu* = `דַּק` (open decision 6); *nakāru* D stative = `מְנֻכָּר` [cognate נכר]; *ina ūmīšū-ma* = `בו ביום`; *alādu* G = `יָלַד`, N = `נוֹלַד`.
11. ***târu ana* "turn into" = `נהפך ל־`** (106 `נהפכה לרמץ`), as flood XI (`נהפכו לטיט`), with the Gen 3:19 contact (`וְאֶל עָפָר תָּשׁוּב`) tagged; `שׁוּב` is not used in IV.
12. **Šamaš / the sun**: *ana pān šamaš* (5; eBL lemma *Šamaš*) and *amāt šamši* (9, 33; eBL lemma *šamšu* "sun") are both written *{d}UTU*; both rendered `שַׁמַשׁ` (the god, George "from Šamaš"), vocalized every time.
13. **Names** vocalized at their first occurrence in the tablet: `הַלְּבָנוֹן` (4), `שַׁמַשׁ` (every time), `גִּילְגָּמֶשׁ` (7; written with yod so that it strips to the plain `גילגמש` of the repeated line), `אֶנְקִידוּ` (10), `חוּמְבָּבָה` (31). `רֵעִי` / `רֵעֵהוּ`, `בֵּרוּ`, `שָׂמוּ`, `שָׂח`, `אֵל`, `הָבֵא`, `כָּרוּ` vocalized every time.
14. **Brackets and quotation marks**: each copy bracketed by its own ATF (decision of guidelines §5); a word with only its ending broken is not bracketed (28 `חלומך`, 30 `ראית`, b+5′ `בכנפיו`). Speech marks open and close only in preserved or restored lines; where the speech runs into a lacuna (55, 109, 142, 183, 74, b+7′, 219) they close as George's do.
15. **eBL slips recorded**: 4 etc. *labnānu* lemmatized *labnu* "flat"; 14 etc. *zuqassu* as *suqtu*; 10 *zaqīqi* as *zīqīqu*; 26, 107 *ṣēri* as *ṣēru* "back"; 73 *immi* as *immu* (George: *emû* "become"); 210 George's passive "have been born" for the active G perfect *ittaldū* (as 213 "sired").

### Open decisions for the user
1. *bīt zaqīqi* = `בית־מַשַּׁב` (chosen; common noun, as XII) or `בֵּית זַקִּיקוּ` (the dream god, George), which sharpens the parallel with `בֵּית אֵל` / `בֵּית אֱלֹהִים` (Gen 28:17, 19)?
2. *šāšû* = `מבולבל` (chosen) or `נָבוֹךְ` (biblical, but not for the dream)?
3. *šarbillu* = `סְעָרָה` (chosen) beside *imḫullu* = `סַעַר` (III 89): two lemmas, one root; alternative `זֶרֶם`.
4. *maḫāru* Š *šuttam* = `מְרַצֶּה לו את חלומו` (chosen; beside *magāru* = `רָצָה` in OB II, a different binyan) or `מְקַבֵּל לו` / `מְיַשֵּׁב לו`?
5. *qablītu* = `אמצע (הלילה)` (chosen) or the exact `הָאַשְׁמֹרֶת הַתִּיכוֹנָה` (Judg 7:19), which brings the root שמר into a `>` line?
6. *qatnu* = `דַּק` (72; collides with *raqqatu* `(אריג) דק`, III) or `צַר`?
7. *maṣḫatu* = `קמח־המַצְחַתּוּ` (transliterated) or `סֹלֶת` [equivalent ↔ Lev 2:1]?
8. *ḫamû* = `קָהֶה` (chosen) or `מְשֻׁתָּק` (closer to CAD "paralysed", modern)?

### Progress log
- 2026-10-18: read the brief, the whole guidelines, corpus, renderings (§9–§19), this project file (coordinator's part, Tablet X), the format models (flood XI, Enūma eliš VI; V of this corpus); ran `parallels_for.py IV --all` (961 lines of output); read eBL IV in full (N, T, L, ATF) and OB Schøyen₂ (N, T).
- Drafted all 175 entries in 12 sections into the data files; generated `annotated-IV.rtl.md` and `translation-IV.rtl.md` (Hebrew introduction, 12 sections, 9 footnotes).
- Pass (a) fidelity: every `>` line read against N and the ATF of its copy; brackets set word by word (e.g. 22, 37, 43, 84–91, 120–129, 162–176 partial brackets by the signs preserved; 1–21, 44–55, 78–83, 130–142, 177–183 fully bracketed); the "…" of eBL's N kept as `…` (12, 13, 25, 157) and `[…]` where the ATF shows a break (91); 109 `יפה?` for *ba?#-ni#*; 156 `הז[את?]`.
- Pass (b) consistency: the canonical strings compared by script with every copy (every difference is in the brackets only, except the 35 listed under "Lines shared", each justified by the Akkadian); XI 301–302 / 319–320 identical in letters (marks only); 211/217 = II 193 / V 125 and 214 = V 73 identical; reserved strings: no `תרדמה`, `קרא`, `פתר`, `פשר` (not needed), `שמר` (no `אשמורת`), `שוב`, `טוב`, `עפר`, `טיט`, `ידע`, `לקח`, `אלוהים`; `שׂים` only for *šakānu* (2, 6, 159 and copies); new Hebrew strings grepped against all earlier `>` lines (none of `מבולבל`, `קהה`, `כרעיו`, `מכמורת`, `מעגל`, `ציור`, `סערה`, `רועשת`, `בוהקת`, `דעכה`, `שלהבות`, `מרצה`, `מנוכר`, `יוכל`, `הניתכת` occurs before). Change: 7 `גִּלְגָּמֶשׁ` → `גִּילְגָּמֶשׁ` (the defective vocalized form made a non-marks difference with 40, 85, 127, 168).
- Pass (c) Hebrew: read through; strip test on the vocalized words. Tchernichovsky 1924 then compared (his IV is in his "Tablet V", from the old German order): he imports `וְתַרְדֵּמָה נָפְלָה עַל אֶנְגִּידוּ` (Gen 15:12 verbatim), `הַאַתָּה קָרָאתָ לִי` (1 Sam 3), `הַאִם לֹא עָבַר אֵל עַל־פָּנֵינוּ` (Exod 34:6), `טוֹב הַחֲלוֹם … וְזֶה פִּתְרוֹנוֹ` (Gen 40:16; 40:12), `הָיָה לְעָפָר מִן הָאֲדָמָה` (Gen 2:7, for the fire turning to embers), `וַיַּשְׁכֵּם … בַבֹּקֶר` (Gen 28:18), `אֱלֹהֵי־הַשֶּׁמֶשׁ`; none is in the Akkadian; nothing changed in ours. His `בָּרַק הַבָּרָק` and `הִמְטִירוּ מָוֶת` agree with ours (cognate and equivalent).
- `python3 -I verify.py IV`: 175 entries = eBL 175, 175 translation lines, **problems: 0**; 35 differences in repeated lines (all justified below), 168 in brackets/marks only.
- `check_bible_quotes.py` on both files and this section: 0 problems (after fixing two references that defaulted to Genesis).

| 12 (= 45, 90, 132, 173) | ušnīlšū-ma ina kippati … uṣurti | השכיב אותו במעגל … הציור | `וְהִנֵּה שָׁאוּל שֹׁכֵב יָשֵׁן בַּמַּעְגָּל … וְאַבְנֵר וְהָעָם שֹׁכְבִים סְבִיבֹתָו` (שמואל א כו:ז) | [equivalent] | The sleeper inside a circle, his companion(s) lying around/at its entrance (13); then `כִּי תַּרְדֵּמַת יְהוָה נָפְלָה עֲלֵיהֶם` (שמואל א כו:יב). *kippatu* = מעגל chosen as the natural word. |

| 14 (= 47, 92, 134, 175) | ina kinṣīšu ūtammeda zuqassu | על כרעיו סמך את סנטרו | `וַיִּגְהַר אַרְצָה וַיָּשֶׂם פָּנָיו בֵּין בִּרְכָּו` (מלכים א יח:מב) | [equivalent] | **The same posture on a mountain top** (Elijah on Carmel). *birku* = ברך is reserved, so כרעיו here (*kinṣu*). OB Schøyen₂ 29. |
| 14 | ina kinṣīšu ūtammeda zuqassu | על כרעיו סמך את סנטרו | `וַיִּקַּח מֵאַבְנֵי הַמָּקוֹם וַיָּשֶׂם מְרַאֲשֹׁתָיו; וַיִּשְׁכַּב בַּמָּקוֹם הַהוּא` (בראשית כח:יא) | [divergence] | Jacob lies down with a stone at his head; Gilgameš sleeps sitting. |

OB Schøyen₂ 34 has Adad (the storm god) for "the heavens". |

| 102 | ūmu ušḫarrir | היום קפא | `וַיִּדֹּם הַשֶּׁמֶשׁ וְיָרֵחַ עָמָד` (יהושע י:יג) | [divergence] | The day "stands still"; *šuḫarruru* = קפא (corpus rule). |
