| b+3′ | eṭla ša tāmuru | העלם אשר ראית | `וַיֵּאָבֵק אִישׁ עִמּוֹ עַד עֲלוֹת הַשָּׁחַר` (בראשית לב:כה) | [equivalent] | The "man" of the vision is the god (OB Schøyen₂ 21: "the one you saw was King Šamaš"); the identification is broken in SB. |
| 215–216 | ša nillakaššu nukkur mimma | אשר אליו אנו הולכים – [מנוכר הוא בכל] | `וַיִּתְנַכֵּר אֲלֵיהֶם` (בראשית מב:ז) | [cognate] | *nakāru* / נכר "be strange, estranged"; restored from OB Schøyen₂ 14–16 (there the interpretation of dream 1: "is it not the mountain?"). |
| 55, 142, 183 | (dreams 2, 4, 5 lost) | – | – | [absent] | The wild bull, the Anzû/thunderbird and the other dream images known from George's reconstruction (from the OB, MB and Hittite witnesses) are **not in the eBL SB text**; b+2′–b+5′ ("whose mouth …", "he will seize its wings") suggest a winged creature in dream 5. Ḫumbaba's seven cloaks (V 99–100, `מעטיו`) and Šamaš's counsel at the forest are not in the preserved lines of IV. |

### Lines shared with other tablets
- **The journey formula**: 1–2 (and 34–35, 79–80, 120–121, 163–164, a+3′–a+4′) = XI 301–302, 319–320, identical in letters (marks-only differences from the brackets). III 173 (`"לעשרים בֵּרוּ [פּוֹת פַּת?]!"`, imperative) ≠ IV 1: justified (*kusup* imperative).
- **X 171 ~ IV 4, 37, 82, 123**: X's head `מהלך ירח וחצי – ביום השלישי` is copied; the rest differs as the Akkadian (*ittalkū* "went" vs *iṭḫû ana šadî labnānu* "drew near to Mount Lebanon").
- **Speech formulas**: 211, 217 = II 193, V 125; 214 = II 216, V 31, 73, 129, 178, 196, 249, 265, 281, 302, 313 (letters identical; own brackets).
- **Within IV, the differences that verify.py lists (35), all justified**: (a) the ordinal of the dream, 21/54/99/141/182 (*ištēt*, *šanīta*, *šalušta*, *rebūta*, *ḫamušta*); (b) the closing quotation mark after 55, 142, 183 (speech running into a lacuna, as George) and the opening mark of 78 (a stand-alone restored speech line) – punctuation only; (c) 165 ends in a full stop: in the fifth cycle the line *mālak arḫi* is absent and the well follows at once; (d) 123–124 are 4 divided over two lines in BabNB3 (the split form); (e) 28 / 109 / 155 differ in what is preserved after *šunatka* (109 `יפה? … […]."`, closing the speech); (f) X 171 (above). The 168 marks-only differences are the brackets of each copy.
- **Fixed here for later use**: the waking questions (18–20), the sleep lines (14–17) and the interpretation close (33) – for the OB Schøyen₂ dreams when that tablet is translated (its 2–3, 29–32 are the same lines; 4b, 30 differ).

### Proposals for the shared renderings
1. **Fixed passage (§3)**: the IV cycle (decision 2) as §3.3, with the plene strings above, for OB Schøyen₂ and any later copy.
2. **Key terms (§4)**: *zaqīqu* = `מַשַּׁב` also in *bīt zaqīqi* = `בית־מַשַּׁב` (open decision 1); *šittu rēḫât nišī* = `השינה הניתכת על האנשים` (with Job 4:13 in the *šittu* row); *šasû* "call (someone)" = `צָעַק אֶל` (1 Sam 3 tagged); *maḫāru* Š *šuttam* = `מְרַצֶּה (לו) את חלומו`; *damqat šunatka* = `נאה חלומך`; *barāqu* / *birqu* = `בָּרַק` / `בָּרָק` [cognate]; *târu ana* "turn into" = `נֶהְפַּךְ לְ־` (add to the *târu* row: the flood corpus's usage, now in IV 106); *nakāru* D stative = `מְנֻכָּר` [cognate].
3. **Ordinary words (§5)**: *ḫamû* = `קָהֶה`; *šāšû* = `מְבֻלְבָּל`; *qablītu* = `אמצע (הלילה)` (never `אַשְׁמֹרֶת`); *kinṣu* = `כְּרָעַיִם`; *kippatu* = `מַעְגָּל`; *uṣurtu* = `צִיּוּר`; *šēšû* = `מִכְמֹרֶת`; *šarbillu* = `סְעָרָה`; *maṣḫatu* = `קמח־המַצְחַתּוּ`; *leʾû* = `יָכֹל`; *mitluku* = `להימלך`; *ramāmu* = `רָעַשׁ`; *nablu* = `שַׁלְהֶבֶת`; *daʾāmu* = `דָּעַךְ`; *belû* = `כָּבָה`; *nebû* = `בּוֹהֵק`; *qatnu* = `דַּק`; *kurrû* = `קָצָר` (used); *ina ūmīšū-ma* = `בו ביום`; *ašar* = `במקום אשר`; *muḫḫu* in *ina muḫḫi* = `על קדקד`.
4. **§7 "always vocalize"**: `קָהֶה` (numb / "blunt" fem.), `כָּרוּ` (dug / "they hired"), `נִפְּחָה` (vs `נַפָּחָה` "smith"), `נוֹלַדְתָּ` (gender), `נָשִׂים` (we put / women), `יִנָּפְחוּ`, `בָּרָק`; and `גִּילְגָּמֶשׁ` with yod when the first occurrence stands in a repeated line (the defective `גִּלְגָּמֶשׁ` fails the strip test against `גילגמש`).
5. **verify.py**: nothing needed. A §3.3 check (the canonical cycle strings against every copy, consonants only, like §3.2 for X) would serve OB Schøyen₂.

### Lessons for the guidelines
- **A tablet built of one repeated cycle should be generated from the canonical strings**: hold each repeated line once, write each copy as the canonical line plus its own bracket pattern, and let the script report every non-bracket difference; the remaining differences are then exactly the Akkadian ones (the ordinal, a missing line, a split line).
- **Fix the Hebrew of a repeated line on its best-preserved copy, not on its first**: in IV the first cycle has no manuscript for 1–21; the Hebrew was fixed on 38, 88–100 and copied backwards.
- **A vocalized first occurrence inside a repeated line must strip to the copies' plain spelling** (`גִּילְגָּמֶשׁ`, not `גִּלְגָּמֶשׁ`), or the script reports a real difference.
- **A reserved root can hide in the exact biblical term of a non-key word** (`אַשְׁמֹרֶת` for *qablītu* carries שמר): check the root, not only the reserved words, before choosing the "obvious" biblical equivalent.
